# Port record

Generated by `gport record` from docs/port/map-v2.json, docs/port/rulings.md, docs/port/dispositions/*.json and docs/port/ledger.md. Each kept block is hand-maintained text between a `gport:kept <name>` line and its matching `gport:end <name>` line. A run copies a kept block's body unchanged, starts a new block empty, and refuses rather than drop a block whose name the layout has no place for. Everything outside the kept blocks is regenerated.

## Direction

This map applies Josh's rulings 1-24. He approved it on 2026-11-05 (ruling 23), and on 2026-11-12 it was brought up to the cut SHA, 0.5.0 at 0256724f1f, and to ruling 24. 134 chunks on 0.9.0, which is the integration branch and the port machine's main checkout (rulings 15, 18 and 24); every one of the 1,094 ledger rows is owned exactly once; every decision has a default or a resolution. TMUX-PURGE comes first (ruling 24, D129): the tmux purge that ruling 8 had on 0.5.0. GEN-ROLLBACK follows (D38): generation-retaining promotion, a window hold, an owner-bound landing freeze and gobby rollback. Phase 0 and the recording wave follow. R0-R6 and SCHEMA-WINDOW land dormant, and CP-RUNNER-REVIEW and CP-RUNNER-CUT switch the daemon to the Rust runner as a one-way switchover (ruling 16); R-RETIRE then removes the Python runner launch path. CP-MCP takes the native gateway live (GW-1, GW-2). CP-TEMPLATE reviews the first route family (A1 and the tasks pilot, with the pilot calibration folded in) and activates it in four cutovers: both families to Compare, then A1 and the tasks reads Native, then the writes, then close and landing. Stage 2 runs in five waves, each closed by a review and a cutover (CP-WAVE3-REVIEW runs the second calibration). Stage 3's verbs, S3-FREEZE and S3-NAME end at CP-PREDELETE, whose cutover makes the live generation Python-free; S3-DELETE follows; CP-FINAL drops the tables that are now dead (ruling 17). Stage 4 lands dormant and goes live at CP-NODE; POST-CLEAN is the last cutover. Every cutover runs nine steps, from its announcement to its rollback recipe. Chunk protocol: gport start (every depends_on task closed as done, and its commit on 0.9.0 except for a review), then claim; merge 0.9.0 into the worktree; one ultracode Workflow; local validation including the dormancy proof; the code-reviewer seat; merge 0.9.0 again; land and push, with no promotion, because only checkpoint cuts promote (D39); close. The port runs on this laptop for now and moves to Josh's Linux machine later (ruling 24, D128). The Game Goblins machine is seeded from this laptop's backup, runs 0.5.0 at the cut and makes no gobby edits; it adopts a checkpoint by its tag when Josh decides (D40).

ORACLE-FIRST, grafted, now with checkpoint cutovers: every chunk's oracle exists or is recorded before its port; every landed commit is dormant through code defaults and promoted only by the next checkpoint cut (D39); each checkpoint is a review pause with Josh followed by a live cutover with a backup, a rehearsed rollback, a bounded window and three outcomes; MCP routes per tool; families are library crates in one gdaemon, rolled back by generation.

Checkpoint model (rulings 14 and 16, confirmed by Josh). A checkpoint is a review chunk (kind checkpoint, category manual, following the manual protocol in its goal: escalate_task, then de_escalate_task after Josh's go, then close_task) followed by one cutover chunk, or four for CP-TEMPLATE. The order: CP-RUNNER, CP-MCP, CP-TEMPLATE, CP-WAVE1 to CP-WAVE5, CP-PREDELETE (its cutover makes the live generation Python-free), S3-DELETE, CP-FINAL (the final post-deletion checkpoint), then CP-NODE for Stage 4 and POST-CLEAN. A review records a packet, escalates to Josh and closes only on his go. A cutover is category code: it owns the activation commit, which gets the code-reviewer seat, together with the prepared deactivation commit, the prepared commits it lands and its approved picks, before the hold is set. Every cutover goal carries nine steps in this order: (1) one global send_message before anything else, naming the backup's restart, the activation restart, the window, its triggers, the landing freeze, the loss window and how a session waits to land; (2) a live read of project_policy.quiet_hours, never copied, starting only when the backup, the rehearsal and the whole window end before the next quiet hours, and a direct read of the protected-runs route that continues only on a 200 answer listing no run; (3) the activation and deactivation commits prepared and given the code-reviewer seat, then GEN-ROLLBACK's hold set, the installed generation pinned by its manifest, the landing freeze set and bound to this task, the cutover branch rebased onto the frozen 0.9.0, then the approved plain picks landed with their pick-ledger update (D40), and the window plan recorded with its trigger table; the pinned generation is the previous cutover's last promotion, which is its activation commit or the last fix-forward or deactivation commit its window promoted, because between checkpoints only an emergency cutover on Josh's word promotes, besides GEN-ROLLBACK's one landing drill (D39); (4) a verified backup with gobby hub-backup, whose stop runs the same protected-run admission as cutover's; (5) a rollback rehearsal of exactly those reviewed commits in an isolated home on a fresh dedicated database, scrubbed of live channels, servers, webhooks and cron jobs, pointed at scratch Qdrant and FalkorDB containers and run on a loopback-only network, where the pinned generation is installed, the reviewed activated build promoted over it, the pinned generation restored and the reviewed deactivation build promoted over that, and where the restored generation serves on the unchanged schema, or on the migrated rehearsal hub when the cutover carries an expand step, and reads back the Rust-written rows, or, for a drop, starts on the restored backup; (6) the activation commit, after the live reads again and with the hold and the pin extended to the window's real end, landed right after the cut's prepared commits (schema inputs, post-TPL templates and the picks that carry either or a gcode or ghook version), which land only here, after the backup and the rehearsal (D22): it flips only that stage's code defaults and carries one 0.0.1 bump per shipped binary whose fingerprint changed since its last bump, gcode and ghook included, with Cargo.lock, the managed pins and the gcode floor in step, is landed under the freeze by the hold's owner, checked against 0.9.0 HEAD, and promoted with gobby cutover --hold-token from an unspawned, operator-opened session through the main checkout's CLI, which also promotes gmcp and gterm when they changed; (7) a bounded window with named triggers, held with a background watch and wait_for_coordination; (8) the outcome, recorded on the task and chosen by the trigger table: it works, and the commit that step 6 promoted is tagged port/<checkpoint id> for A to adopt (D40); it works well enough to debug, so the session fixes forward with the pin and the hold extended, for at most two fix cycles, the last landing within 24 hours of the first outcome-2 decision, unless Josh's go set another bound, and once the clean window after the last fix passes, the last promoted commit is tagged the same way; or it rolls back and Josh reassesses, while in-flight dependent chunks stop at their next checkpoint; (9) the rollback recipe: gobby rollback restore of the pinned generation with its named members (gdaemon alone by default, the stamped set after a schema step), then the prepared deactivation commit, which also reverts the cut's schema inputs, landed under the freeze and promoted, so an expand step is undone with no backup restore (D22); a backup restore comes first only for a drop (CP-FINAL-CUT, POST-CLEAN) or CP-WAVE3-CUT's relocation, after every live agent seat is ended, with the seats relaunched once the restored daemon is healthy. Every recipe names only install-local state, so A runs it unchanged if its own adoption of a checkpoint rolls back; A adopts by running the cutover's steps on its own install, with its main checkout moved to the tag in step 6's place after its own backup and rehearsal (D40). Post-deletion cutovers (CP-FINAL-CUT, CP-NODE-CUT and POST-CLEAN) run the same steps through V1A's Rust verbs. Windows are serialized: each cutover depends on the one before. The template fan-out waits on CP-TEMPLATE-CUT; each later wave's first chunks wait on the previous wave's review, so building overlaps the previous window. The landing freeze holds their landings only from that cutover's step 3 to its close; a landing outside that span lands dormant (D37), and on outcome 3 the cutover's project message stops them (A7-WAVES). Per-checkpoint scope, switches, rollback targets and windows are in the checkpoint table.

(1) Python edits on 0.9.0 (D4): tests/ and scripts/ for recording, and scripts/port/ for the port's checks; the R0-R6 worker package and runner edits; GEN-ROLLBACK's promotion, rollback, landing-freeze-owner, hub-backup admission and protected-runs answer modules; R-RETIRE's runner refusal; one-line allow-listed worker-op registrations for single-owner services, added by the Rust caller's chunk; TERM-1's dormant terminal route helper; GW-2's dormant loopback compatibility endpoint; SCHEMA-WINDOW's preflight edit, a prepared commit that CP-RUNNER-CUT lands; S3-NAME's dormant gobby-name installer constant, which CP-PREDELETE-CUT's commit A turns on, and its packaging edit in commit B; S3-DELETE; and bug fixes and friction fixes (ruling 6), which land on 0.9.0 like everything else (ruling 15). Every edit other than a bug fix, TMUX-PURGE's purge (D129), R-RETIRE's refusal and GEN-ROLLBACK's generation-retaining promotion is dormant (D4): behaviorally inert at landing, because new CLI processes and lazily imported modules run it at once. GEN-ROLLBACK's retention is live at landing, so the generation each cutover pins is already retained. No per-family deletion, no Python client of a Rust endpoint, no modernization. (2) Still-Python families calling a ported family keep calling the Python implementation in-process over the shared tables, and differential replay of each family's corpus proves the two equivalent. Rust reaches a still-Python family only through its existing HTTP or MCP surface or an allow-listed worker op; Python never calls Rust. (3) Shared tables (D19): one schema chain, the same lock keys and notify channels, Rust tolerates every Python-written row. Coexistence is live from CP-RUNNER-CUT on (runner job, recovery and lease rows), and from CP-MCP-CUT for the gateway's schema lease rows. A value gdaemon hands to Python, such as GOBBY_TERMINAL_ROUTE, is set only by gdaemon's compiled defaults, and Python follows the latched value (TERM-1's helper), so an activation flips only gdaemon's default. (4) Worker ops: the Rust side stops calling an op at the activation that replaces it, and no registration is removed before S3-DELETE, because pinned and retained generations still call it. S3-FREEZE asserts that the activated build calls none. (5) Deletion (D14, ruling 14): Python stays whole until S3-DELETE, which runs after CP-PREDELETE-CUT has made the live generation Python-free; until then every retained generation still finds its Python worker code in the checkout. (6) Live-checkout constraints: no template moves before S3-DELETE; a template edit only as A17-TEMPLATES allows, with its rule-17 bump and, after TPL, a gdaemon bump and a landing inside the next checkpoint cut, as a prepared commit in its step 6; expand-only migrations, as prepared commits that the cut which activates their chunk lands in its step 6 (D22 as resolved); Python edits dormant, with D4's exceptions; 0.9.0 restartable at every landed commit and promoted only at checkpoint cuts (D39 as resolved).

0.9.0 is the integration branch on the port machine (P), and P's main checkout sits on it (D40). Each chunk works in its own task worktree off 0.9.0 (for example 0.9.0-rust) and lands with ordinary land_commit. Nothing merges into 0.9.0 from another branch, and D1's option A is not built. Python fixes, POST-CLEAN and Stage 4 all land on 0.9.0. 0.5.0 freezes at the cut SHA (ruling 15). The other machine (A, the Game Goblins machine) runs that SHA from a restored copy of P's hub and makes no gobby edits (ruling 24), so no branch forks from 0.9.0, no fix flows from A, and the pick steps that every review and cutover carries have nothing to list (D40 item 3). A adopts a checkpoint when Josh decides, in checkpoint order, by running that cutover's steps on its own install with its main checkout fast-forwarded to the port/<checkpoint id> tag in step 6's place, after its own backup and rehearsal (D40 item 4). The start gate moves project_policy's integration branch to 0.9.0 on P; it rewords AGENTS.md rule 15's prose (AGENTS.md:146) while its dated operator ruling stays verbatim, rewords rule 10, and makes the 0.9.0 runs of ci.yml and rust-ci.yml required on push and on pull requests (D34, D85).

The live daemon runs from the 0.9.0 checkout, so every landed commit must keep it working:

- No template moves before S3-DELETE.
- A template edit lands only as A17-TEMPLATES allows: a ruling 6 bug or friction fix, or a rewrite that preserves behavior on Python's engine, such as RULE-1A's. The live daemon must accept it unchanged, it carries its rule-17 bump, and once TPL declares the template tree as an embedded input it also bumps gdaemon and is a prepared commit that the next checkpoint cut lands in its step 6, under that cut's backup, and that cut's activation promotes it (ruling 20).
- Migrations are expand-only until CP-FINAL-CUT (D22). A chunk's schema inputs (its migrations with their assets.rs entries, and the tests that need the new schema) form one prepared commit on the append-only branch port/prepared/<chunk id>, reviewed with the chunk and recorded by SHA at its close; the first checkpoint cut whose activation needs that schema lands it in its step 6, after the verified backup and the rehearsal and immediately before the activation commit, under the same freeze. Between cuts no landing changes the schema pin, because gobby restart and hub-backup refuse while the checkout's pin differs from the installed identity (src/gobby/storage/schema_divergence.py:251-265, via src/gobby/cli/daemon_preflight.py:68-78). The preflights accept a cut's migrations only with a pin generated from a real build, a regenerated catalog manifest, transactional migrations inside the 5 s lock and 60 s statement limits, serial numbers and no edited applied migration. From CP-RUNNER-CUT on every retained generation accepts a hub ahead by expand-only receipts (SCHEMA-WINDOW), so a rollback across an expand step is the binary restore plus the deactivation commit; only a drop, at CP-FINAL-CUT or POST-CLEAN, needs the backup restore.
- Python edits are dormant (D4), except bug fixes, TMUX-PURGE's purge (D129), R-RETIRE's refusal and GEN-ROLLBACK's generation-retaining promotion. That promotion is live at landing, so the generation each cutover pins is already retained.
- Every later pinned generation finds its Python worker code in the checkout, because nothing in Python is deleted before S3-DELETE, no worker-op registration is removed before it, and worker protocol changes are additive.
- 0.9.0 stays restartable at every landed commit and promotable at every checkpoint cut: a landing bumps only gdaemon, between checkpoint cuts no landing changes the schema pin, the gcode or ghook floor or, after TPL, a template, and only a checkpoint cut promotes (D39).

Families are library crates on gcore inside one gdaemon (D36). GEN-ROLLBACK keeps three generations, each with a manifest (D38). A cutover sets a hold whose token every promotion and restore in it must pass (gobby rollback hold set, then --hold-token on gobby cutover, gobby install and gobby rollback restore), pins the generation installed at that moment, and sets a landing freeze bound to its own task, so land_commit admits only that task's session. gobby cutover also promotes gmcp and gterm when their versions changed. A restore names its members (--bin, gdaemon alone by default, and always the stamped set gcode, gdaemon and ghook for a cut that changed the schema identity) and burns the activated versions, never the restored target's own. Dormancy is code defaults only (D37). From CP-RUNNER-CUT on, every retained generation accepts a hub ahead by expand-only receipts (SCHEMA-WINDOW), so a rollback across an expand step is the binary restore plus the deactivation commit, which reverts the cut's schema inputs with its activation, and loses no write. A rollback across a drop (CP-FINAL-CUT and POST-CLEAN only), or across CP-WAVE3-CUT's attachment relocation once it ran, is a backup restore first, after every live agent seat is ended, with the seats relaunched once the restored daemon is healthy, and it loses every write made after the backup. Every cutover recipe uses only install-local state (its own verified backup, the same schema inputs, its own pin, hold and generations), so A runs that whole cutover on its own install when it adopts a checkpoint, with its main checkout moved to the tag in step 6's place after its own backup and rehearsal (D40).

## Reference base

Cut SHA: `0256724f1f`, named by the map summary.

## Chunk protocol

1. Run step 3's gport start check first, from the main checkout, and claim nothing while it refuses. Then claim the chunk task in a default-agent session, spawned by the port dispatcher or operator-opened (D39, D116), in a task worktree off 0.9.0 at the launch base that check recorded. An operator-opened session makes that worktree with gobby-worktrees:create_worktree(branch_name=<branch>, base_branch='0.9.0', task_id=<task>), because git worktree add and the provider's own worktree tool are refused in it (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/worker-safety/block-git-worktree-mutations-interactive.yaml:5; src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/worker-safety/block-native-enter-worktree.yaml:5); its own directory stays the main checkout, so it works in the worktree by absolute path and names that path in every Workflow agent's prompt. A session the dispatcher spawned finds this check done and this claim made, and starts at step 2. If get_worktree_by_task (gobby-worktrees) finds a worktree for this task, an earlier session on it stopped or died. The stale claim sweep releases a dead session's claim (src/gobby/storage/tasks/_automation.py:89); until it has, get_task names the owner, and claim_task(force=true) takes the claim once get_session shows that owner is no longer active. Reuse that worktree, settle its uncommitted files, and resume after the branch's last checkpoint commit, rebuilding any lost scratch report from the branch. If that session's candidate already landed, only step 8's close remains.
2. Merge 0.9.0 into the worktree, and rerun differential replay for every touched family that is ported or in flight. From P0-RECORD's landing on, read docs/port/record.md next: this chunk's Found Work section, its family's debt-not-to-port list and owned rows, the frozen seam contract section and the known-bad index, because step 4 builds on them. Read this chunk's Found Work section again after every later merge of 0.9.0: a seam change or a fix task may have added a line to it (step 9, D40 item 3), and this chunk handles that line before it lands.
3. gport start <chunk id>, run before the claim as step 1 says: every depends_on chunk's Gobby task is closed with closed_reason done, and for every kind except checkpoint its linked commit is an ancestor of 0.9.0 HEAD; it records the launch base. Until P0-RECORD lands gport, check the same rule by hand with get_task. gport is never promoted, so it is not on PATH: run it as cargo run --locked -q -p gobby-port -- <arguments>, in the main checkout for start and in the worktree for check-slice and ledger, because a path-invoked ./target/debug/gport counts as script execution, which the task gate refuses a session with no claim (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-enforcement/require-task-before-edit.yaml:18). A task outside the map, such as a review's no-go fix or a fix or port between reviews (D40 item 3), is filed as a child of #21542, so the port dispatcher starts it (D116). It has no chunk id, so gport start and gport check-slice refuse it: it checks its dependencies by hand with get_task, skips check-slice at step 6 and follows every other step. A fix that completes a closed chunk's row updates that chunk's docs/port/status/<chunk id>.json with its evidence line, because D17's rate reads that file.
4. Run one ultracode Workflow (at most 16 subagents): the debt-not-to-port pass, the oracle recording first when the brief says so, adversarial design review, tests rewritten from contracts, implementation, adversarial code review.
5. Use 1-3 checkpoints, each a commit on the chunk branch plus a scratch report. A chunk with ledger rows keeps docs/port/status/<chunk id>.json, one entry per row (P0-RECORD); a row marked done names a red commit and a later green commit on this branch, so commit each failing test before the code that passes it, because gport check-slice refuses a done row without both SHAs in its range. While the Workflow runs, the session yields with wait_for_coordination(background_task_id=<the Workflow's provider task id>, timeout=3600), omitting owner_session (src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/coordination.py:45-49), and registers it again after every timeout wake and after every prompt; after a compaction it fetches the wait tool's schema again first. A stop message that names this chunk, from a review's no-go or a cutover's outcome 3, holds the session at its next checkpoint, landing nothing, until the matching release message arrives; it waits with wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<the dispatcher>, reply=true, timeout=3600), registered again after every timeout and every prompt. It does not wait on the sender, because a review's fixes outlast the session that sent the stop, and a wait on a session that has ended returns owner_ended at once and leaves this run to the idle check (src/gobby/agents/idle_check_handler.py). The release is a project send with wake=true, which reaches a session holding a claim whichever session it waits on.
6. Validate locally: cargo tests, the isolated full-daemon harness (never the live hub, never ~/.gobby/bin), the dormancy proof (code defaults replay every existing corpus unchanged), gport check-slice <chunk id> --task <this task's ref>, and gport ledger, or scripts/port/check_coverage.py until gport ledger's diff is 0. Managed SRT changes what some checks prove inside a spawned session: a test that launches an agent under SRT fails there, because macOS refuses a nested profile (tests/e2e/conftest.py:490-498); a process check can miss what it may not inspect (docs/guides/gterminal-development-guide.md:445-453); and a proof that a non-loopback connection fails passes there whatever the code does. Docker is refused there too (src/gobby/agents/srt_settings.py:63-64). Send such a check's test command and this worktree's path to the port dispatcher with send_message(target='parent'), register wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<the dispatcher>, reply=true, timeout=3600) as step 8 does, and cite the reply in the validation attestation (D116).
7. Spawn the independent code-reviewer seat on the task worktree with spawn_agent(agent='code-reviewer', worktree_id=<this worktree's id, from get_worktree_by_task>, claim=false, prompt='Review <full SHA> of <task ref> in <worktree path>. Send your verdict and findings with send_message(target=parent). No pipeline, lead or claim hand-off applies here; land, merge and close nothing.'). It takes no task_id, as Josh's 2026-11-03 rule says, so the claim stays with this session, and claim=false joins the tree at its HEAD, because without it the spawn merges the base into the tree, which moves the candidate, and refuses a tree that a live session has claimed (src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_implementation.py:470, 512-529; src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_worktree_reuse.py:57-116, 119-149; tests/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/test_associated_worktree_join.py:28). The seat starts on the provider project_policy names, whose CLI must be on P (D119). Wait with wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<the seat>, reply=true, timeout=3600), registered again after every timeout and every prompt. When a wait ends without a reply and gobby-agents:list_running_agents no longer shows the seat, spawn it once more with the same arguments; a seat still listed after three such waits counts as lost once stop_agent ends it. A second loss escalates the task to Josh, and once he says the seat can start, run de_escalate_task and claim_task and resume at this spawn. Fix and recheck, stop it, then record the seat's result and the candidate SHA on the task as an attestation. Send nothing to a session on A: the coordinator that Josh's 2026-11-03 rule names, gobby#19309, has expired, the fleet seats beside it ended before the cut, and no chunk depends on cross-machine messaging (D40 item 5). If Josh opens a coordinator seat on P, send the SHA to it as well; the security-auditor seat on P takes only the candidates named below. A candidate whose changed paths match project_policy.security_paths, whose task carries the security-review label or whose brief names a security review, a gated chunk under D106 among them, also waits for the security-auditor seat that Josh opens on P (D115), because D115 asks for that seat's independent approval of the exact SHA. When this map was approved, land_commit refused that SHA without the seat's approved security-review attestation and a spawned default-agent session could not spawn the seat itself; since #25900 default's capabilities ['*'] waive those rules for a default-definition caller and cover the seat's in spawn-capability-authority, the rule that refused the spawn, so the wait is this protocol's own rule (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-closure/security.yaml:25-60; src/gobby/workflows/agent_models.py:37-51; src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml:5). The wait runs as follows: after step 8's merge and version bump and before land_commit, send it the task ref and the resulting commit's full SHA, with target='session' and the session ref that gobby-agents:list_running_agents gives, then register wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<the seat>, reply=true, timeout=3600) and register it again after every timeout and every prompt. On a rejection, fix, rerun the code-reviewer seat and send the new SHA; every changed SHA needs its own approval. If a landing during the wait changed a path this candidate also changes, and the gdaemon version line usually is one, merge 0.9.0 again, bump again and send the new SHA before land_commit, because land_commit refuses that candidate (step 8). When list_running_agents shows no live seat, at the send or after a wait that ends without a reply, escalate the task to Josh, who reruns D115's command, and land nothing until the seat has approved. Escalation releases the claim, so once Josh says the seat is live, run de_escalate_task and claim_task and resume at the send.
8. Merge 0.9.0 into the worktree again. A chunk session lands and bumps, and never promotes (D39): on P it never runs gobby install, gobby cutover or gobby rollback restore (GEN-ROLLBACK's live drill and TMUX-PURGE's leaves are the planned exceptions, D39 and D129), because gobby install rebuilds and promotes gdaemon whenever its managed pin is above the installed version, which every landing's bump makes true (src/gobby/cli/install_setup_gdaemon.py:79-112, 141-170), and rebuilds gclient, gmcp and gterm on every run; its landing bumps gdaemon by 0.0.1 when its fingerprint changed since its last bump, and leaves gcode, ghook, gmcp, gclient and gterm to the next activation commit, because the gcode and ghook pins are runtime floors (src/gobby/code_index/gcode_versions.py:5-6; src/gobby/hooks/runtime_compat.py:17, 28). A chunk that carries schema inputs or, after TPL, a template change lands its dormant code here and puts those inputs and its own tests that need them, validated with that code in the isolated harness, in one prepared commit on port/prepared/<chunk id>, recorded by SHA at close, which the checkpoint cut that needs it lands in its step 6 (D22). A chunk whose code or tests need a schema that a depends_on chunk's prepared commit carries, and that lands before the cut that lands that commit, applies the upstream prepared commit (its SHA is on that task's close record) only to its isolated harness tree, which for a spawned session is a local scratch branch in this worktree because its write scope stays on this worktree, never to its landing candidate, and puts its own tests that need that schema in its own prepared commit on port/prepared/<chunk id>, based on the upstream one and recorded by SHA at close; the same cut lands it right after the upstream commit. CI on 0.9.0 never runs a test that needs a schema 0.9.0 does not carry. During a landing freeze, send the cutover session a direct message, then register wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<cutover session>, reply=true, timeout=3600) and register it again after every timeout and every prompt; the cutover session messages back when the freeze clears. The merge and the bump make a new candidate SHA, and land_commit refuses a candidate whose implementation, test-after and validation attestations name another SHA (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-closure/coding.yaml:116-127). So commit them, link that commit, rerun step 6's checks on it, and record those three attestations on its full SHA, implementation before test-after (:86-93). No coding rule reads a self-review attestation since #25852, so the session records none, and step 7's attestation of the seat's result stays the record of the review. The seat runs again only when the merge resolved a conflict outside the version lines. Then land_commit onto 0.9.0 and push 0.9.0. A spawned session can neither push nor read CI, because spawn_agent runs it under managed SRT with no git network and no git or gh credentials (src/gobby/agents/sandbox_gate.py:22-33; docs/guides/sandboxing.md:245-249), so it sends the landed SHA to the port dispatcher with send_message(target='parent'), registers wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<the dispatcher>, reply=true, timeout=3600), sends again after a timeout with no reply, and registers the wait again after every prompt; the reply is its CI result (D116). Check the pushed commit's CI, then record the closing attestation and close_task with that attested SHA, never a generated land merge's, because a validation attestation takes the landing-receipt exception only from a receipt that carries land_commit's own verified version bump (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-closure/coding.yaml:95-114), which land_commit generates only when the landing branch is named 0.5.0 (src/gobby/tasks/land_commit.py:750-774; src/gobby/tasks/closure_snapshot.py:37-70). If land_commit refuses with base_update_required or merge_conflict, because another landing moved 0.9.0 through a path this candidate also changes, it has also escalated the task and released the claim (src/gobby/tasks/land_commit.py:469-484, 714-727, 742-744; src/gobby/storage/tasks/_transitions.py:417-456), so this session resumes it itself: de_escalate_task, claim_task, then this step again from its merge, with the attestations the new SHA needs.
9. Found work follows AGENTS rule 4. From P0-RECORD's landing on, a line goes under the Found Work section of the chunk it concerns in docs/port/record.md, in the commit that fixes it; gport check-slice passes a Python or frozen seam path outside this chunk's slice only when a Found Work line under this chunk names that path. A seam contract change is found work of this kind: it lands on 0.9.0 in the owning seam file, with any landed caller it breaks fixed in the same commit so that 0.9.0 stays green, and in-flight chunks pick it up when they merge 0.9.0 (steps 2 and 8).

A checkpoint review instead follows the manual protocol in its goal (escalate_task, then de_escalate_task after Josh's go, then close_task), and a cutover chunk follows its nine steps. A corpus or golden case that a plan gives a waiver citation keeps its recording until the activation commit that flips its surface, which lands the citation: a citation makes the case fail while the old answer serves, so it would fail step 6's dormancy proof. Until then the chunk's own replay reads a fixture copy of the entry and citation in its tests, which tells the cut what to land (D48). Where a family plan, a task card or other pre-map text disagrees with a map decision or a chunk's explicit text, the map wins; docs/port/plan-drift.md lists the disagreements known at approval, and a chunk that finds another records it under Found Work. Where the map is silent, the plan applies. A map choice that proves broken is a gap: report it through Found Work instead of working around it from the plan. No chunk landing adds an entry to docs/contracts/parity-waivers.json or changes the parity ledger, even where a family plan's leaf says it appends its entry: the entry lands with its citation in the activation commit that flips its surface, because each entry writes a Waived note on a ledger row that the old answer still serves (scripts/generate_parity_ledger.py:190-194) and gdaemon embeds the manifest (crates/gdaemon/src/front_door/waivers.rs:12) (D84). A CI run that a later push to 0.9.0 cancelled (.github/workflows/ci.yml:16-18) is superseded, not red: step 8's check records that and goes on, because the later push's run covers the commit, and a red result there is found work for the session that landed it (D85). Plan short names in briefs: s2-11 is .gobby/plans/s2-11-hub-hooks-rules.md; S2.7 is .gobby/plans/s2-7-hub-attention-dispatch.md; sessions, the sessions plan and sessions-family are .gobby/plans/sessions-family.md; memory and the memory plan are .gobby/plans/memory-family.md; code-index is .gobby/plans/code-index-family.md; agents-family is .gobby/plans/agents-family.md; tasks-family is .gobby/plans/tasks-family.md; the AI plan is .gobby/plans/gdaemon-ai-providers-family.md; the Projects plan is .gobby/plans/gdaemon-projects-files-docs-family.md; the multiplexer plan is .gobby/plans/external-mcp-transport-multiplexer.md; gmcp-stdio is .gobby/plans/gmcp-stdio.md; hub-mode is .gobby/plans/hub-mode.md; DR1-DR8 are in .gobby/plans/rust-rule-evaluation.md; and #25341's numbered sections are in .gobby/plans/task-25341.md. A plan's text that a later Python fix changed does not apply: on that point the port follows Python at the chunk's base, because its corpora record that behavior, and the session notes the plan's stale text under Found Work. A usage limit or a provider outage is not a red result and closes nothing, and the branch stays. A spawned session that one stops loses its pane and its claim once the watchdog ends the run with a provider terminal reason (src/gobby/agents/lifecycle_monitor.py:668-688; src/gobby/agents/idle_check_handler.py:386-395), and the replacement the port dispatcher starts afterwards resumes under step 1 (D116). An operator-opened session keeps both: a usage limit holds its pane on the provider's menu, where no wake lands until Josh submits a prompt there after the reset (src/gobby/hooks/event_handlers/_misc.py:81-85, 415-416), and after an outage its next wake or prompt reaches it. Whichever session resumes reruns the stopped Workflow from the branch's last checkpoint commit (step 5). crates.io or GitHub down is an outage too, so retry after the next wait. A brief over 15,000 characters comes back from get_task as an offload envelope whose preview holds only its first 2,000: read the whole brief with gobby-results:get_tool_result, following next_offset until it is null, and call get_task again after the 7-day retention (src/gobby/config/features.py:127-143; src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/results.py:149-188). A port carries every trust check in the module it ports, named in its brief or not: whatever refuses, bounds, redacts or confines untrusted input, with Python's negative tests replayed as native cases, because a recorded corpus holds no attack and a missing check passes replay. A native HTTP client keeps its Python client's policy: httpx follows no redirect by default and reqwest follows ten, so each client sets redirect::Policy::none() unless its Python source sets follow_redirects=True, and trust_env=False becomes .no_proxy(). A native route that reads a body states its limit, because axum's body extractors stop at 2 MiB by default and Starlette has no limit. A native child whose Python spawn passes env= calls env_clear() and sets that map, because Rust's Command inherits gdaemon's environment (D124). A chunk that reuses existing native code lists Python's steps on that path against the native code's before its oracle runs, and ports each missing step or reports it under Found Work, because an oracle that compares native output with native output proves nothing about parity (D124). Josh approved this map on 2026-11-05 with every default (ruling 23), so a decision status that waits on his word at map approval is settled.

## Registration protocol

<!-- gport:kept registration -->
Source: the P0-RECORD chunk goal in docs/port/map-v2.json. gport check-slice enforces the rules marked (checked); the rest are kept by the chunk session and its reviewer.

- Append-only: the family list, the workspace members in the root Cargo.toml (checked: a removed member is refused), the gdaemon dependencies in crates/gdaemon/Cargo.toml (checked: a removed key is refused), the nextest overrides and the gclient verb table.
- Cargo.lock is regenerated after merging 0.9.0.
- Migrations and node-commands contract versions take the next free number at landing.
- Edits to serve.rs, the front_door/websocket refactor and front_door/auth.rs are serialized.
- Version rule (D39): a landing bumps gdaemon when it changed gdaemon's fingerprint, and never promotes. gcode, ghook, gmcp, gclient and gterm are bumped only in a cut's shipping commits (its activation commit, each outcome-2 fix commit and its deactivation commit) and in emergency cutover commits (checked: a changed package version in those crates is refused for every chunk whose kind is not cutover). Only checkpoint cuts promote.
- Every brief records the pushed cut SHA under Reference base as its reference base. gport start records each chunk's own launch base by printing it (protocol step 3).
- A task outside the map (a pick, or a schema fix between reviews, D40 item 3) has no chunk id: it skips gport start and gport check-slice and follows the rest of the protocol.
<!-- gport:end registration -->

## Decisions

### D1: Landing mechanism for chunk tasks on 0.9.0.

**Default:** 0.9.0 is the integration branch, and the main checkout moves to it (ruling 15). Chunk tasks land with ordinary land_commit onto 0.9.0, because land_commit and its git hook target the main checkout's current branch (src/gobby/tasks/land_commit.py:153-159; src/gobby/cli/installers/git_hooks.py:106), and P's main checkout sits on 0.9.0. The start gate also moves project_policy's integration branch from 0.5.0 to 0.9.0 (.gobby/workflows/rules/project-operating-policy.yaml:33, with a rule-17 bump), which only seat procedures and the injected policy text read. Each chunk works in its own task worktree off 0.9.0, for example 0.9.0-rust. Option A (a subtree landing target) and option B (close at cutover) are not built, and nothing merges into 0.9.0 from another branch: P's Python fixes, POST-CLEAN and Stage 4 all land on 0.9.0, a gobby fix that Game Goblins needs is a fix task on P, and A commits nothing: it adopts a checkpoint by moving its checkout to the tag (D40). The landing session pushes 0.9.0 to origin after each land, so the port branch always has an off-machine copy. A cutover session is unspawned and pushes for itself after each landing it makes; a spawned session's push goes through the port dispatcher (D116).

**Rationale:** Josh: 'we're not merging back into 0.5.0. 0.9.0 will become the new working branch we'll merge into, and you'll be working in a worktree like 0.9.0-rust'. Ordinary landing keeps land_commit's landing lock, ancestry and clean-tree checks, all five close gates per chunk and blocked_by readiness, with no new landing code. The cost is lost isolation: the live daemon runs from the 0.9.0 checkout, so every landed commit reaches it at the next restart, which the live-checkout constraints and dormancy (D37) address. Order between chunks comes from one blocks edge per depends_on entry among the chunk tasks, which readiness honors, and from gport start, which refuses a chunk until every depends_on task has closed as done and, except for a review, its commit is an ancestor of 0.9.0 HEAD.

**Resolved by:** ruling 15

### D2: Comms order (#25597 puts comms right after R0 and its seams; the 2026-10-25 edges make comms wait) and the close of #22340.

**Default:** COMMS-1 in wave 3 after R5, AG-3 and its recorded corpus, with sends through a worker op, live at CP-WAVE3-CUT; COMMS-2 (adapter connections, inbound webhooks, attachments, identities) in wave 5 after the last Python in-process sender, live at CP-WAVE5-CUT. #25597's early order is not adopted. The adapter set follows D28 (rulings 12 and 13). #22340, folded into #24173 on 2026-10-28, closes done citing COMMS-1's landing SHA in place of the #24173 plan SHA its escalation names, because no such plan lands. #25597 closes as wont_do in D31's pass, because its order is not adopted.

**Rationale:** #25597 put comms early so that a broken comms port could roll back to the prior binary. GEN-ROLLBACK's retained generations now give every checkpoint that rollback (D38), so the early order buys nothing. Comms has no oracle until P0-LOWTRAFFIC records one, and adapter connections are single-owner state that cannot run in two implementations.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D3: Cookie verifier #24967 and #24659 versus nine Stage 2 consumers.

**Default:** Build #24967 now, in SEAM-CALLERS before the pilot; #24659 lands in CIX-1 on that verifier.

**Rationale:** Nine consumers need it, and the after-hub-node deferral predates decision 26.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D4: May chunks edit Python on 0.9.0 (record oracles, delete ported families, add facades)?

**Default:** Only: tests/ and scripts/ for recording, and scripts/port/ for the port's checks (start gate); the R0-R6 worker package and runner edits; one-line allow-listed worker-op registrations for single-owner services, added by the Rust caller's chunk; GEN-ROLLBACK's promotion, rollback, landing-freeze-owner, hub-backup admission and protected-runs answer modules (D38); R-RETIRE's runner refusal; TERM-1's dormant terminal route helper (D19); GW-2's dormant loopback compatibility endpoint (D26); S3-NAME's dormant gobby-name installer constant, which CP-PREDELETE-CUT's commit A turns on, and its prepared packaging edits, made in commit B; SCHEMA-WINDOW's preflight edit (src/gobby/storage/schema_divergence.py, reached through src/gobby/cli/daemon_preflight.py only at restart, cutover, hub-backup and pack), a prepared commit that CP-RUNNER-CUT lands (D22); S3-DELETE; and bug fixes and friction fixes, which land on 0.9.0 (rulings 6 and 15). The Rust side stops calling a worker op at the activation that replaces it, and S3-DELETE deletes the op, because pinned and retained generations still call it. Every Python edit other than a bug fix, TMUX-PURGE's purge (D129), R-RETIRE's refusal (inert for the gdaemon-launched worker, which always carries the marker) and GEN-ROLLBACK's generation-retaining promotion (live at landing, so the generation each cutover pins is already retained) is dormant: behaviorally inert at landing, because new CLI processes and lazily imported modules run it at once. No per-family deletion of surfaces or domain modules, no Python client of a Rust endpoint, no modernization (af4be945). Also allowed, each under its own decision: D41's closure-policy op and shim; D47's fence half (src/gobby/projects/write_fence.py, src/gobby/storage/hub/postgres.py, src/gobby/runner_init/services.py), a prepared commit; D67's communications status op and shim; D68's restart start-proof entry in R6's worker package; D70's read of R5's start list in src/gobby/servers/_app_lifecycle.py's pending-interaction expire; D74's composer_locked_write op and shim; D75's handoff delivery op and shim; D81's web chat lifecycle op and shim; D82's after-tool phase in GW-2's preflight op and shim; D98's edit of src/gobby/servers/_app_lifecycle.py's three loop starts; D99's calls to the existing track_proxy_outcome and maybe_repair_output in GW-2's op shim and compatibility endpoint; D103's terminal_operator_write op and shim; D105's terminal_focus_changed op and shim; D110's agent_runtime_spawn op and shim; D121's daemon-owned execution and pipeline execution id fields on GW-2's compatibility endpoint. Each is dormant at landing or lands as a prepared commit, as its decision says, and S3-DELETE deletes it. D64's project.json fix sits in the bug-fix clause.

**Rationale:** Keeps Python whole and equal to its recorded behavior, so differential replay stays valid and every retained generation still finds the Python code it calls. Worker ops are the cheapest correct path for single-owner services while their Python callers remain (ruling 9).

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D5: Re-homing the 254 umbrella #21568 rows, the 15 rows on closed tasks and the ownerless rows.

**Default:** The map's ledger selectors are the re-homing. gport ledger writes chunk ids into the owner column at the cut. Delegated, dropped and unclassified rows are owned by the chunk that confirms them.

**Rationale:** check_coverage.py shows all 1,094 rows owned exactly once; one mechanism replaces a manual re-cite pass.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D6: #24359, #24360 OAuth 2.1 scope, #19652 transcript archive, #22075 classifier.

**Default:** #24359 (gmcp as a published managed binary) moves to S4-RELEASE as a leaf, and the #23275 to #24359 edge is deleted with the move. #24360 (OAuth 2.1 for the HTTP transport) is post-1.0: Josh already ruled CIMD only (2026-10-13), so the only open question is whether port-as-is defers new capability, and it does. #22075 (deferred-by-user) is post-1.0; #19652 has no deferral label, so Josh confirms its post-1.0 move at approval and its D31 disposition records it. #19652, #22075 and #24360 leave the #21542 tree in D31's pass, with no close.

**Rationale:** No ledger row needs #24360: local-first 1.0 uses operator, agent-token and grant callers, and node-to-hub uses enrollment credentials. A bare move of #24359 would leave a Stage 2 holder waiting on Stage 4, so the edge goes with it.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D7: gobby auth login: port it, or drop it for gobby node enroll (#24863, #24861)?

**Default:** Drop the CLI verb, as #24863 already decided (node-install-enrollment D12): V1B records the ledger drop before S3-NAME, and #24863 closes as superseded. The web UI HTTP login stays (AUTH). S4-RELEASE builds gobby node enroll (#24861). From CP-PREDELETE-CUT, where the Python CLI leaves, until CP-NODE-CUT activates S4-RELEASE, no CLI enrolls a new node; enrolled nodes are unaffected, and CP-PREDELETE-CUT's smoke proves the existing one.

**Rationale:** One enrollment path. Because Stage 4 now follows CP-FINAL-CUT (D24) the gap is visible for that window, but it only affects enrolling a new node; Josh can instead keep gobby auth login in V1B until CP-NODE-CUT.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D8: New crate dependencies hdbscan 0.12.0 and kdtree 0.7.0 (#25546), and the embedding serving lease (#25542).

**Default:** Already approved: Josh approved the stamped memory plan that contains both (attestation aca21b08 on #25479, 2026-11-02). The map places them: hdbscan 0.12.0 and kdtree 0.7.0 exact-pinned and only in the memory crate (MEM-2) after a licence check; #25542's serving lease in AI-2 as the single embedding-serving owner for MEM-2, CIX-2, MCP-FLIP and MUX-1's tool vector writer, its projection-change ledger included, which MEM-2's native writers append to.

**Rationale:** Review refuted the needs-Josh reading. Reclustering parity needs HDBSCAN, and one lease owner avoids three competing collection rebuilds during an embedding switch.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D9: #21588 voice engine and Crane TTS licence (reserved by Josh).

**Default:** Josh decides before VOICE starts (VOICE is scheduled after CHAT-2 in wave 3). D107 is the answer VOICE builds unless he rules otherwise at map approval.

**Rationale:** Josh reserved the call; the trait keeps the engine swappable without blocking the surfaces.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D10: #21564 runbook-seat MCP surfaces admit_team and check_runbook_seats: port or drop? (#25828 removed the third, cold_start_admin, before the cut.)

**Default:** Port them, with get_team and team_progress, in AG-5 after PIPE-2. Per-tool routing (D26) lets the pilot soak gobby-tasks tools while get_team and team_progress stay proxied until AG-5.

**Rationale:** Bundled team pipelines depend on them, so dropping them breaks runbooks when Python stops serving at CP-PREDELETE-CUT. admit_team runs only from a team pipeline's admit step, so it follows PIPE-2. Admission policy stays declared in YAML (AGENTS rule 16).

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D11: Owners for POST /api/nodes/report and the node runs rows (#21564 or Stage 4 #25269).

**Default:** NODEHUB (Stage 2, wave 4) owns report, checkouts, runs/* and detection-manifests and absorbs #25269's hub side. HOOK-3 owns nodes/hooks/replay and nodes/receipts, because it is the chunk that dispatches hooks natively. Stage 4 builds the node side against them. Its report and terminate routes, with the reported pane store, the node report inbox and the run reconciler, activate at CP-WAVE5-CUT with the terminal host, the store's last Python in-process reader (D19); remote idle's decision is AG-2's native idle check over the store's facts, and until CP-WAVE5-CUT AG-2 reaches Python's reconciler and store through worker ops.

**Rationale:** The exit rule needs every Python surface delegated before #21574, which precedes Stage 4, and a row-owning chunk upstream of the native dispatch core would need a Python hand-off. It also overrides the Projects plan's transfer of POST /api/nodes/checkouts from #24151 to Projects (.gobby/plans/gdaemon-projects-files-docs-family.md D1, 4.5.5 and 6.3.5, recorded under #24951 on 2026-10-24): NODEHUB keeps that row with the other node endpoints this decision gives it, and its checkout handler calls PROJ-2's native checkout service with 4.5.5's contract, so the service keeps one owner.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D12: #21559 materialize_managed_launch and the rotate_due loop: the runtime plan keeps them in Python, but Stage 3 deletes Python.

**Default:** Port both in A1-RUNTIME: materialize_managed_launch as a gruntime service, and rotate_due's native iteration, which A1-RUNTIME registers on R4's single scheduler itself. A1-RUNTIME now depends on R4, which reverses the earlier map's R4-on-A1-RUNTIME edge. The job registers only when the runtime family is active, so it starts at CP-TEMPLATE-READS-CUT.

**Rationale:** S3-DELETE removes Python, so nothing can stay there. Ruling 16 makes the runner a firm switchover that must cover R0-R6 before CP-MCP and the first family, and its notes move R4's native rotate_due registration into A1-RUNTIME, so CP-RUNNER's scope carries no A1 code and the runner is not held for the pilot.

**Resolved by:** ruling 16

### D13: What is the pilot's compare soak?

**Default:** Two stages. First, CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW runs the bounded in-session replay soak: three passes of P0-HARNESS's soak loop over the committed REC-PILOT corpus, weighted by the gport shapes census, then differential and dual-run write replay of the whole corpus in the full-daemon harness, judged by D17. Then CP-TEMPLATE-CUT runs the live Compare day: the tasks and runtime families run in Compare on the live daemon for one full active day, so callers always get Python's answer (crates/gdaemon/src/front_door/routes.rs:186-254) while gdaemon compare-report scores real traffic. A 72h soak runs only if Josh asks, as a detached process between chunks with no claim held. The real-data corpus (a pg_dump of the hub DB without secrets tables, restored into the test hub, never committed) stays off unless Josh approves it. CP-WAVE3-REVIEW's soak is bounded the same way, with an optional detached 24h run. Later families always get differential replay, and each wave's cutover window is its live observation.

**Rationale:** Josh's pilot asks for 'a full compare soak'. Ruling 14's checkpoint cutovers make a live Compare window possible with no new machinery: Compare already gives callers Python's answer and logs every diff, so the live day adds real interleavings and agent load that a replay cannot see, at no risk to callers. A 72h soak inside a chunk session would hold a claim for days on waits bounded at 3,600 s (#25635), and #25460 v2 had already made it optional. The snapshot copies private data, so Josh opts in.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D14: When is ported Python deleted, given the 1,172-module lazy-import cycle?

**Default:** All at once in S3-DELETE, after S3-FREEZE and after CP-PREDELETE-CUT has made the live generation Python-free; no per-family deletion of surfaces or domain modules. Flagged: this reverses approved per-family deletion leaves (memory #25562 and #25563, code-index #24709, #24710 and #24713, cron #25035, s2-11 P7, #25499, and #24356's wrapper deletion; the memory plan with per-family deletion was approved on 2026-11-02). Each becomes 'stop serving and freeze' at its family's activation, and the deletion lands in S3-DELETE.

**Rationale:** Josh: 'Yes, it can be deleted in one go, or we can strangler fig it.' One go is the cheaper of the two here: every pinned and retained generation before CP-PREDELETE-CUT still runs Python from the checkout, so a per-family deletion would break generation rollback (D38). Differential replay against Python is the drift oracle until S3-FREEZE. Still-Python callers reach MCP descriptors and managers in-process. Partial deletion inside the 1,172-module lazy-import cycle breaks untested lazy-import paths at runtime. Unreachable Python routes are harmless while the family's backend is Native.

**Resolved by:** ruling 14 permits it; one go is the map's default

### D15: Homes for port tooling, bundled templates and pin authority.

**Default:** crates/gport (dev-only, never promoted) replaces the Python ledger generator and the scripts/port/ checks, and holds start, check-slice, census and shapes. gport start refuses a chunk until every depends_on task is closed with closed_reason done and, for every kind except checkpoint, its linked commit is an ancestor of 0.9.0 HEAD. A gobby-assets crate embeds the template tree at build time (build.rs include_bytes!, no new dependency) and moves it to assets/shared at S3-DELETE, with TPL as the single loader and sync owner. Pin and promotion authority, with GEN-ROLLBACK's generation store, window hold and rollback verb (D38), moves to Rust in V1A. #24999's asset script is not built, and S3-FREEZE copies the SRT inputs into gobby-assets for S4-INSTALL's #24866.

**Rationale:** Each must outlive Python. Embedding keeps templates version-locked to the binary, which serves AGENTS rule 17.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D16: Who owns the 330 operator CLI rows?

**Default:** Stage 3 verb chunks V1A-V5B over the frozen public API, after their families, with REC-CLI goldens as the oracle. Family chunks add no gclient verbs. A verb with no endpoint behind it adds one in the family crate through Found Work. The memory CLI is a gclient verb set too: #25555, #25556 and #25560 build no gdaemon memory host and no Python forwarder. D69 is the exception for verbs that must work with no daemon running, such as gobby sync and gobby install's content step: they reach the hub through a gdaemon subcommand.

**Rationale:** ROADMAP decision 24 and tasks-family Q6 (#21571). Clients come after stable APIs, and it keeps the pilot's measurement clean.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D17: Calibration gate thresholds for CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW and CP-WAVE3-REVIEW.

**Default:** Proceed when the D11 rate (partial+stub)/(done+partial+stub) is at most 5% of owned rows, the final replay pass has zero unexplained compare mismatches with every mismatch class triaged (fixed, known-bad, or an accepted contract change), and differential replay is 100% equal or classified. Cost data (session hours, tokens, checkpoints, Rust test lines and Python lines per checkpoint, recording cost per case, and cost against relief) resizes later chunks. The gate's result goes into the review packet. A failure is a no-go: a follow-up commit and a re-run of the replay soak, and a second failure means re-planning with Josh. D11 here and in the checkpoint packets names .gobby/plans/task-25341.md's fan-out gate (:131-135), not this map's D11; gport check-slice --report prints the rate from each chunk's status file (P0-RECORD).

**Rationale:** Josh's pilot asks for a full compare soak and a measured partial/stub rate before fan-out, and thresholds turn the measurement into a gate. #25460 v2 had made the D11 gate and the 72h soak paperwork optional after B0. This map keeps the D11 gate before fan-out because its angle is oracle-first, runs the bounded replay soak of D13 in place of a 72h soak, and keeps the paperwork to gport's D11 report plus one compare-report summary.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D18: Known-bad policy for Python-recorded oracles.

**Default:** Rust implements the correct value, never python_observed. Shell-guard items in the friction queue are fixed first (start gate); the rest are registry rows. A fix that lands on 0.9.0 later retires its row in the same commit and re-records the affected cases. A row covers a difference below any one ledger surface; a difference on one exact surface takes a docs/contracts/parity-waivers.json entry instead (D84).

**Rationale:** Josh item 4; parity means contracts, not accidents (settled item 5).

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D19: Coexistence rules while Python and Rust implementations share 0.9.0.

**Default:** Shared DB tables may have two implementations: the corpus proves equal DB deltas, Rust tolerates every Python-written row, both use the same lock keys and notify channels, and Rust emits the events Python caches listen for. Single-owner live state has one owner at a time and moves after its last Python in-process caller (callers before callees). Rust calls Python only through existing endpoints or allow-listed worker ops; Python never calls Rust. A stricter invariant needs a DB constraint Python already satisfies. Because the main checkout runs 0.9.0 (ruling 15), coexistence is live from CP-RUNNER-CUT on (runner job, recovery and lease rows), and from CP-MCP-CUT for the gateway's schema lease rows, not only in tests. A value that gdaemon hands to Python, such as the worker marker or the terminal route GOBBY_TERMINAL_ROUTE (crates/gdaemon/src/state.rs:162-171), is a child-contract value set only by gdaemon's compiled defaults, and Python follows the value gdaemon latches: TERM-1's dormant helper makes both of Python's terminal route reads use the latched value (src/gobby/runner_backend.py:83; src/gobby/runner_init/terminal_wiring.py:64-68), so an activation flips only gdaemon's default and a gdaemon-alone restore hands the old value back in one restart. If Josh refuses that Python edit, the activation flips Python's two reads with gdaemon's default, and the rollback lands the deactivation commit under the freeze as the hold's owner, then restores the pinned generation, then promotes the deactivation build under the token, because Python refuses to start when the two disagree (src/gobby/runner_backend.py:80-91); any other value gdaemon hands Python needs the same order. For project files the shared lock is the owner-only sidecar flock that Python's project initialization takes (src/gobby/utils/durable_file.py:26-38), and for derived writes scoped to a project it is gcore's project fence, which Python's ProjectWriteFence takes through its Python half (D47). S3-DELETE deletes TERM-1's helper with the rest of src/gobby.

**Rationale:** Answers the cross-family and shared-table questions without any Python client of a Rust endpoint and without dual-mode machinery, and keeps every retained generation's Python able to start against the checkout it finds.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D20: Rows dropped at ledger closure.

**Default:** Drop the FastAPI docs, redoc and openapi rows (8; the Rust API is documented in docs/contracts), test-types and test-quality CLI (5; no Python tests remain), /api/admin/test/* (4; a harness feature), and /_gobby/ws/bridge (1; dies with Python). Keep the recorded drops #25449 (4), #19319 (1) and tasks compact and clean (#25499, 5). Josh approves each class. D66 adds one more class: gobby sessions renumber and backfill-context-windows (2).

**Rationale:** None of these is a product surface once Python retires.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D21: #25269, #25201 and #21576 would reverse approved Stage 4 placements.

**Default:** Move their Python-surface parts to Stage 2 owners: #25269's hub side to NODEHUB, #25201 to TERM-1 (keep hub-mode's remote write branch), #21576 to COMMS-2 on PROJ-2's files_home documents. Node-side parts stay in Stage 4. #25269's hub side activates at CP-WAVE5-CUT, and its remote idle decision runs in AG-2 (D11).

**Rationale:** S3-DELETE removes their Python surfaces before Stage 4 starts. Review flagged the reversal as needing Josh.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D22: Migration policy on the live 0.9.0 checkout.

**Default:** Migrations are expand-only until CP-FINAL-CUT: a migration adds tables, columns or indexes that Python ignores, together with the GRANT and REVOKE statements on the objects it creates. Schema inputs (migrations with their assets.rs entries, baseline.sql, the pin and the catalog manifest) land only inside a checkpoint cut, in its step 6: after the verified backup (4) and the rehearsal (5), immediately before the activation commit, under the same freeze; the rehearsal builds from the cutover branch, where those commits sit under the activation commit. They cannot land before the backup, because hub-backup's restart start-half proof refuses while the checkout's pin differs from the installed generation's schema identity (src/gobby/cli/hub_backup/cli.py:228-235; src/gobby/storage/schema_divergence.py:251-265, via src/gobby/cli/daemon_preflight.py:68-78), and between cuts the same check would refuse every session's restart. Each schema-carrying chunk (B0-WRITE, SKILLS, CRON, PLANS and PROJ-2, and SES-2 only if Found Work gives it a schema input, because the sessions plan adds none) lands its dormant code at its own landing and closes; its schema inputs and the tests that need the new schema form one prepared commit, reviewed with the chunk, on the append-only branch port/prepared/<chunk id>, recorded by SHA at its close, and the first cut whose activation needs that schema, Compare included, lands it. Dependent chunks apply it only in their isolated harness. The carrying chunk's own tests that need its schema travel in its prepared commit, and a dependent that lands before that cut puts its own schema-dependent tests in its own prepared commit, which the same cut lands after the upstream one (chunk protocol step 8). The cut assembles its branch before the rehearsal: the prepared commits in packet order, each migration renumbered to the next free number, except one that a rollback reverted, which keeps its number and bytes (below), one commit regenerating baseline.sql, the catalog manifest and the pin from a real build, then the activation commit; the deactivation commit reverts all of them. Step 6 lands the whole sequence with one land_commit on the activation SHA, because land_commit refuses a landing that changes migrations, baseline.sql or assets.rs without src/gobby/storage/schema_expected_identity.json (src/gobby/tasks/landing_policy.py:76-91). If the cut's promotion refuses after they landed, the session lands the deactivation commit under the freeze before clearing it, so every session's restart passes again (ruling 15). From CP-RUNNER-CUT on, every retained generation accepts a hub ahead by expand-only receipts (SCHEMA-WINDOW), so a rollback across an expand step is the binary restore plus the deactivation commit, which reverts the cut's schema inputs so that the checkout's schema identity matches the restored generation, with no data loss; a later cut re-lands the same migration file in its step 6 (the retry after Josh's reassessment, or the next cut that needs it), whose receipt then matches. Drops, and every other step outside the expand set, are destructive migrations, carrying the -- gobby:destructive directive and the filename marker that SCHEMA-WINDOW pairs with it and lints for. They happen only at CP-FINAL-CUT and POST-CLEAN, each an irreversible step with its own go from Josh, and rollback there is that cut's backup restore. The other rules stay: each migration transactional, with no transaction control, inside the 5 s lock and 60 s statement limits (crates/gcore/src/schema/runner_plan.rs:56-92); numbers taken serially at landing, each above both the checkout's highest migration and the highest receipt on the live hub (SELECT max(version) FROM schema_migrations), because an expand migration that a rollback reverted keeps its receipt; such a migration keeps its number and bytes for good: it is re-landed byte for byte even if its direction is abandoned, and any correction is a new migration; applied migrations never edited, except that the deep rollback to Python replaces each drop migration with a no-op of the same number on a restored backup that predates it (CP-PREDELETE-REVIEW); tests run with GOBBY_TEST_GDAEMON=checkout; the pin generated from a real build (src/gobby/cli/daemon_preflight.py:119-137); the catalog manifest regenerated (src/gobby/cli/cutover.py:286-318). Nobody stops and starts to get around a restart refusal. Node-commands contract versions take the next free version at landing, so no Stage 2 plan chains onto a Stage 4 version. From S3-DELETE on, this regeneration commit has no Python generator and changes less. Numbered migrations apply above baseline@420 and leave baseline.sql as it is (crates/gcore/src/schema/assets.rs:554), so the commit does not touch it; the catalog manifest regenerates through UPDATE_GCORE_SCHEMA_MANIFEST (crates/gcore/tests/catalog_manifest_freshness.rs:399); and the pin stays at src/gobby/storage/schema_expected_identity.json, the one file S3-DELETE leaves under src/gobby, written by gdaemon schema version --json | jq -S ., which produces the bytes src/gobby/storage/schema_identity_pin.py:79-81 writes today. S3-DELETE deletes SCHEMA-WINDOW's preflight edit (D4) with src/gobby/storage/schema_divergence.py.

**Rationale:** Josh: 'each rust cutover should include a database backup, in case we're making changes that can't be easily rolled back (like removing a lot of dead tables/columns from the database found as tech debt during the port)'. On rollback versus migrations (2026-11-03): 'We should probably figure out a workaround'; on promotion: 'promote binaries on checkpoints right? we don't want to run a full rust recompile every time a binary is updated'. Expand-only keeps Python working on the migrated schema. Before SCHEMA-WINDOW, every older gdaemon refuses a migrated hub, because gdaemon checks the schema identity exactly (crates/gdaemon/src/main.rs:276-297; crates/gcore/src/schema/runner_plan.rs:153-169), so a rollback across any schema step would need the backup restore and lose the writes made since; SCHEMA-WINDOW narrows that refusal to destructive receipts, so only the drops keep that cost, and they keep their own go. Landing schema inputs only inside cuts, after the backup, keeps every session's restart and hub-backup working between cuts and puts each schema change under a rehearsal, a window and a recipe. Ruling 20's 'a schema-carrying chunk lands in its checkpoint's cut' is met by the chunk's schema inputs. Its dormant code lands and closes at its own landing, because a chunk that waited for its cut would block its own dependents (B0-CLOSE needs B0-WRITE, CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW needs B0-CLOSE, and CP-WAVE1-REVIEW needs SKILLS, CRON, PLANS and PROJ-2), and gport start admits only dependencies that are closed as done.

**Resolved by:** Josh, 2026-11-03/04, rulings 19 and 20

### D23: How many chunk sessions run at once?

**Default:** At most 2 chunk sessions until CP-TEMPLATE-CUT closes, not counting the one review or cutover session that holds the current checkpoint. The runner, gateway and pilot paths share those 2. The second session starts only after P0-RECORD lands, so the registration protocol is in force before it starts. After CP-TEMPLATE-CUT, up to 4, set from CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW's measured provider quota, landing-lock wait, and code-reviewer throughput (Josh's 2026-11-03 rule puts that seat on every candidate), plus security-auditor throughput (protocol step 7, D115), and re-set at CP-WAVE3-REVIEW. The 18 CPUs, 128 GiB and 4.7 TiB free measured for this map are this laptop's, where the port starts (ruling 24). The port may have moved by then (D128), so CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW also measures P's CPUs, memory and free disk, and the cap stays within them. Landing stays serialized by the landing lock. While a cutover holds the landing freeze, other sessions keep building but do not land. The port dispatcher applies the cap (D116). A session that a stop message holds does not count (D116).

**Rationale:** Each session runs up to 16 subagents, the pilot must measure one chunk's cost cleanly, and the real bottlenecks are quota, the landing lock and the review seats, not hardware.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D24: Where does Stage 4 run relative to Python's retirement?

**Default:** After CP-FINAL-CUT. All eight S4-* chunks depend on CP-FINAL-CUT, land dormant on 0.9.0 (ruling 15), bump gdaemon at landing and are first promoted by CP-NODE-CUT's activation (D39), and go live together at CP-NODE-CUT, a checkpoint this map adds for Stage 4, whose review waits on S4-PROOF. POST-CLEAN follows CP-NODE-CUT. Fallback if Josh wants node work live sooner: activate each S4 chunk at a checkpoint of its own instead of CP-NODE-CUT.

**Rationale:** Decision 26 orders Stage 4 after Python retirement, which this keeps. One Stage 4 checkpoint keeps the live node on its existing gdaemon until the whole node path is proven on isolated daemons (S4-PROOF). Ruling 15 removes the hand re-port window that a merge into 0.5.0 would have opened.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D25: Where does Josh's A1 (#21559 P4 #24452-#24457 and P5 #24458-#24473) run, and does #21560 keep its edge on #21559?

**Default:** Before B0, as A1-RUNTIME, A1-CONFIG and A1-SECRETS (#24452 in SEAM-CALLERS), so Josh's pilot order holds: Phase 0, then A1, then B0, then the soak. The #21560 to #21559 edge stays (ROADMAP S2.4a after S2.3, kept in Josh's 2026-10-18 split). The seam wave is renamed SEAM-FAMILY, SEAM-CALLERS and SEAM-WS, so 'A1' means Josh's A1 only.

**Rationale:** #25341 D1 defines A1 this way, and review calls the edge an ordering choice for map approval. The cost is three chunks on the pilot path; CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW then calibrates on config and tasks together.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D26: How does the MCP gateway route tools while families port one at a time?

**Default:** Per tool: every server:tool has its own backend (Native, Compare or Proxy). gcore's ToolRegistry composes several family crates into one server and rejects only duplicate tool names. A2 splits into GW-1 (native /mcp, routing, composition, discovery, leases) and GW-2 (REST tool routes, per-tool Compare, the preflight op and the compatibility endpoint). Option (a), the default and the contract's choice: the gateway owns the single policy pass (docs/contracts/internal-mcp.md:82, 86-88). GW-2 adds a dormant, loopback-only, secret-bound Python compatibility endpoint that dispatches each Proxy tool against Python with no policy pass, listed under D4. Proxy tools route to it, and whenever the gateway serves /mcp (in the harness, and live from CP-MCP-CUT's activation) it runs GW-2's preflight worker op for every tool until HOOK-3's joint flip (D27). Option (b), the fallback if Josh refuses the endpoint: the gateway runs the preflight op only for Native and Compare tools, Proxy keeps Python's /mcp and its own pass, CP-MCP-CUT says 'the policy pass for Native tools moves to the gateway', and GW-1 amends docs/contracts/internal-mcp.md:86-88. Map approval approves the #24841 plan as amended against runner-first, which settles its criterion 3. The endpoint answers for every Proxy tool, which includes external servers through Python's MCPClientManager until CP-WAVE5-CUT moves them to MUX-1's Backend, and the gobby namespace's Python tools until their chunks flip; it still runs no policy pass and exposes no external configuration route (GW-2). S3-DELETE deletes the compatibility endpoint.

**Rationale:** ToolRegistry::new returns DuplicateServer when a second family names the same server (gcore mcp.rs:191-195), so per-server routing cannot soak the tasks tools while agents, plans and team tools on shared servers stay Python. At ~15K lines A2 was also too large for one session. Option (a) is the contract's own design: one policy pass, in the gateway, for every tool, so no call skips the pass or runs it twice; forwarding Proxy tools to Python's own /mcp would run Python's pass on top of the gateway's.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D27: When do hook-time rule passes and MCP tool-call rule passes move to Rust?

**Default:** Together, in HOOK-3, under s2-11's hooks_mcp_split guard: one CLI session's hook passes and tool-call passes share one engine and one FIFO (leaf 7.15.5). GW-2's preflight worker op stays until then, and RULE-2 no longer switches preflight on its own. In the map that guard is one compiled default that drives hook ingress and the gateway's tool-call rule-pass engine together, with a test that no build exposes them apart; 7.15.5's start refusal keyed on front_door.routes values is not built (D37), and GW-2's op carries the after phase until the same flip (D82).

**Rationale:** A split flip would run two engines over one session's FIFO and break the ordering s2-11 requires (plan :87, :3159-3179).

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D28: Which comms adapters ship in 1.0? The registry loads seven: slack, telegram, discord (652 lines), teams (348), email (600), sms (274) and gobby_chat (205).

**Default:** One gobby-comms adapter contract in Rust that every adapter implements (connect, inbound, outbound, attachments, identity), defined by COMMS-1 and implemented by COMMS-2. Telegram, Slack, Teams, email and SMS ship in 1.0. Each existing Python adapter is verified first against a real test account (a send, an inbound message and an attachment) before its recording can serve as an oracle; an adapter that verifies is ported as-is, and one that fails is ported from its provider's published API contract instead. Teams reverses #24173's Teams drop. Discord ships in 1.0 if its stated test passes: a live round trip on a test guild covering connect, an inbound message, an outbound message, reconnect after a gateway disconnect, and an attachment; otherwise its move to after 1.0 is recorded as a deferral: a post-1.0 task filed after the friction queue (ruling 10), with Discord's recorded fixtures and test-guild script kept. iMessage is after 1.0. gobby_chat is dropped: its sends deliver nothing (docs/reviews/comms-integrations.md:83-84), the web UI already hides it (web/src/components/activity/integrations/IntegrationsTabModel.ts:54; web/src/components/activity/integrations/channelMetadata.ts:3), and its seeded channel rows stay inert until CP-FINAL-CUT can delete them. P0-LOWTRAFFIC fakes every shipped adapter at the network edge.

**Rationale:** Josh: 'The goal is to have a unified gobby-comms interface that works with Telegram, Slack, Teams, email, SMS, iMessage, etc. Not sure what gobby_chat is or how it's used currently. Might be legacy code.' and 'Discord if we can make it work, otherwise defer to after 1.0.' He does not know whether the adapters work, so verification comes before porting. The ledger has no adapter rows, so the coverage check passes whatever is decided, and an unported adapter would silently vanish at S3-DELETE.

**Resolved by:** rulings 12 and 13

### D29: Who owns the session mailbox (messages, attachments, waits)?

**Default:** AG-3 owns messages and attachments (including the get_attachment and send_attachment rows) and delivered-mailbox retention; AG-WAIT owns waits and the completion registry. Comms and cron consume AG-3's family API, and gcron never deletes inter_session_messages rows itself (#25033, which closes as superseded by CRON's task under D31), and R4's registry schedules that retention until AG-3's native job replaces the op. One interim exception before AG-3 activates: A1-RUNTIME's adapter writes the capability_expired agent_recovery row, a single shared-row insert under D19, until CP-WAVE2-CUT's activation commit replaces it with AG-3's writer.

**Rationale:** #24173 criterion 8, tasks-family and agents-family all claim the mailbox, and the cron plan's 2026-10-24 ruling assumed #22340 was still parked. One owner per table API follows crates/AGENTS.md.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D30: Who runs the cutover?

**Default:** Each cutover chunk's own session, a default agent (ruling 11), running as an unspawned, operator-opened root interactive session: a spawned agent is refused (src/gobby/releases/activation.py:21-32), a CLI run from a linked worktree is refused (src/gobby/utils/dev.py:134-153), and a headless spawned run cannot register wait_for_coordination for the window (src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/coordination.py:64-69). It runs the main checkout's CLI with --path at the main checkout. Its brief carries the nine cutover steps: the global announcement; the restart timing, with project_policy.quiet_hours read live at that moment, never restated, and a 200 protected-runs answer that lists no run; the hold, the pin and the landing freeze bound to its task; the verified backup; the rollback rehearsal; the activation commit with its version bumps; the window; the decision; and the recipe.

**Rationale:** Josh: 'we're not building this with runbook agents, so a default agent (like you) would handle it'. The session constraints come from the code: activation refuses spawned agents, the CLI refuses linked worktrees, and the window needs a live wait. Quiet hours are policy that changes, so the brief reads them live instead of copying a window that can go stale. #25718 gave restarts and cutovers a dedicated seat in the fleet that ran before the cut, at the Orchestrator's direction.
