### D106: How does a chunk session on P clear the security gates its landing meets? Landing and closure refuse a candidate that carries the security-review label or touches a project_policy.security_paths entry until a definition declaring approve_security_review records an approved security-review attestation on its exact SHA, unless the caller's definition holds waive_review_gates: since #25900 both rules test that first, and default's capabilities ['*'] hold it (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-closure/security.yaml:25-60; src/gobby/workflows/agent_models.py:37-45; src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml:5; the rules are selected by src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml:117; .gobby/workflows/rules/project-operating-policy.yaml:35-265, which lists crates/gdaemon/src/websocket/**). Plans add that gate on their admission or landing leaves, most after an Orchestrator applies the label (.gobby/plans/gdaemon-ai-providers-family.md:1172-1181; .gobby/plans/gdaemon-skills-family.md:85-88, 1834-1846; .gobby/plans/gdaemon-cron-family.md:1564-1568; .gobby/plans/tasks-family.md:2042-2050; .gobby/plans/memory-family.md:2294-2307; .gobby/plans/s2-11-hub-hooks-rules.md:2896-2899; .gobby/plans/sessions-family.md:216-225; .gobby/plans/hub-mode.md:448-454; .gobby/plans/hook-envelope-file-facts.md:314-317), and A1-RUNTIME's risks require that review for #25068. No chunk task has an Orchestrator, protocol step 7 sends nothing to a session on A, and when this map was approved a spawned default-agent session could not spawn the seat. Since #25900 spawn-capability-authority no longer refuses it: default's spawn scope is every definition, so spawn-capability-authority compares capabilities, and default now declares capabilities ['*'], which covers every definition's (src/gobby/workflows/condition_helpers_spawn.py:103-123; src/gobby/workflows/agent_models.py:48-51; src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml:5).

**Default:** D115's seat serves every gated chunk. A gated chunk is one whose brief cites this decision, one whose family plan gates its work on a security review, or one whose candidate touches a security path. Its session adds the security-review label with gobby-tasks:add_label right after its claim, in place of the plans' Orchestrator step, or as soon as it sees that its candidate touches a security path. After protocol step 8's merge and version bump, and before land_commit, it finds the seat with gobby-agents:list_running_agents, sends it the task ref and the resulting commit's full SHA, and waits with wait_for_coordination(owner_session=<seat>, reply=true, timeout=3600), registered again after every timeout. The seat reads the candidate by that SHA, which the shared object store holds, and records the attestation itself, with the full SHA as its git reference (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/security-auditor.yaml). The rules compare that exact SHA, so a later merge or fix gets a renewed run, and a rejected candidate is fixed and sent again. With no seat running, a chunk session escalates its task to Josh and lands nothing, and a cutover session follows its step 3 (D115). A plan's activation or admission record names this label and attestation, and no pre-dispatch check exists. The next review packet lists each attestation by SHA. Alternative with no standing seat: Josh opens each gated chunk's session himself, so that operator session, which passes spawn-capability-authority because it is unspawned (src/gobby/workflows/agent_resolver.py:68-70), spawns the seat as step 7 spawns the code-reviewer, which gave up spawned sessions for those chunks when this map was approved; since #25900 spawn-capability-authority passes a spawned default-agent session too (D106's question).

**Rationale:** The rules and the seat exist and need no code, and an independent authorized approval of the exact SHA is what the plans' gates ask for: only who applies the label and who opens the seat change. The claimant may add the label: add_label checks only claim authority (src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/tasks/_lifecycle_labels.py:22-35; src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/tasks/_authorization.py:87-116), and block-update-task names only update_task and is not among default's selectors. Protocol step 7's rule against sending to A stays (D40 item 5). When this map was approved, a gated landing was refused without the seat and its session could do nothing about it; since #25900 the security rules pass a default-definition caller's landing without that attestation (D106's question), so the seat's attestation is the independent approval the plans' gates ask for and no longer one land_commit enforces on such a caller.

### D107: D9 leaves VOICE's engine to Josh, and its default (the current engine behind a trait, no Crane TTS) cannot be built: the current speech-to-text is the faster-whisper Python package (src/gobby/voice/stt.py:31-44; pyproject.toml:54), the default text-to-speech is chatterbox-tts on torch (pyproject.toml:76-79; src/gobby/config/voice.py:79), VOICE edits no Python, and S3-DELETE deletes Python. Which engines does VOICE build?

**Default:** VOICE builds these engines in a new crate, crates/gvoice (package gobby-voice, publish = false), behind one speech-to-text trait and one text-to-speech trait. Speech-to-text: whisper-rs, exact-pinned at 0.16.0 and only in that crate, with its metal feature on macOS, serves the reserved whisper provider: transcribe and translate, with whisper_prompt and whisper_vocabulary as the initial prompt and segments in Python's result shape (src/gobby/voice/stt.py:116-226). It is a cfg(unix) target dependency, because Windows stays post-1.0 (83a9445f) and MUX-1's compile-only Windows clippy job cross-lints gobby-daemon from ubuntu-latest, where a native C or C++ build finds no MSVC headers, as .github/workflows/rust-ci.yml:152-154 records for libghostty-vt; a Windows build has no whisper engine and its probe returns false. voice.whisper_device set to cpu turns the GPU off and any other value leaves it on; voice.whisper_compute_type has no whisper.cpp meaning, so native ignores it. Ports of src/gobby/ai/audio.py's capability service, adapters and builder (:107-362) take each audio binding from crates/gai's capability snapshot, and the port of OpenAICompatibleAudioAdapter (:278-334) serves voice.openai_compatible_audio's bindings. whisper_model_size names the whisper.cpp ggml model, stored as <gobby_home>/models/whisper/ggml-<size>.bin and downloaded from huggingface.co/ggerganov/whisper.cpp on first load, as faster-whisper fetches its model today. Audio of Python's accepted types (src/gobby/voice/stt.py:168-176) decodes to 16 kHz mono PCM through the ffmpeg binary, which Telegram voice notes already run (src/gobby/communications/tts_voice.py:64), so no decoder crate enters. #25072's probe returns whether ffmpeg resolves, because the engine is compiled in and a missing model downloads on first load, just as Python's probe reports only that its runtime is installed (src/gobby/voice/stt.py:228-234). Criterion 1 stands, criterion 3 is the contract change D50 names, and the reason crates/gai reports on false changes from Python's faster-whisper text (src/gobby/ai/registry_builder.py:276) to 'ffmpeg is not installed.' through the Found Work AI-1B's risks allow. Text-to-speech: a port of src/gobby/voice/tts_crane.py, over HTTP to voice.tts_crane_url with its v1/audio/speech PCM16 stream and ready check. Crane stays a process the operator runs (docs/guides/crane-tts.md), any server with that contract works, and gdaemon links no Crane code and no candle. chatterbox is dropped: native reads voice.tts_provider's chatterbox value as crane, and any other unknown value keeps Python's unknown-provider status (src/gobby/voice/providers.py:24-27). Python's voice classes stay unedited in the worker for Python's Telegram path until CP-WAVE5-CUT, and S3-DELETE deletes them with the faster-whisper and chatterbox-tts dependencies. CP-FINAL-CUT sets voice.tts_provider's default to crane and removes voice.whisper_compute_type and the four keys only Chatterbox reads, as it removes code_index.maintenance_log_file. If Josh declines whisper-rs, speech-to-text goes only through voice.openai_compatible_audio, with crane-serve's POST /v1/audio/transcriptions as the default binding: pure Rust with no C++ in gdaemon's build, but no built-in engine, so the whisper provider reports unavailable. If he wants Crane in process inside gobby-voice, that is a further implementation of the same trait after 1.0.

**Rationale:** Josh on #21588: 'The STT engine should be rust-based' (2026-10-25) and 'I want to replace chatterbox and any other TTS with our own implementation of crane' (2026-10-23); on #23768: 'we need to replace chatterbox with crane'. The default follows PB11 (.gobby/plans/task-25341.md:67-70: whisper-rs, any OpenAI-compatible /v1/audio/transcriptions endpoint, Crane or any /v1/audio/speech endpoint, chatterbox dropped), whose operator origin Josh confirms with this answer. Crate check on crates.io, 2026-11-04: whisper-rs 0.16.0, published 2026-03-12 (0.15.1 on 2025-09-10), Unlicense, repository codeberg.org/tazz4843/whisper-rs, one publisher (tazz4843), about 1.0M recent downloads, a metal feature and no declared rust_version; it wraps whisper-rs-sys 0.15.0, which builds whisper.cpp from source with cmake and bindgen as build dependencies, so building gdaemon on macOS and Linux gains cmake, a C++ compiler and libclang, and 'rust-based' here means Rust bindings over C++. GobbyAI's Crane fork is MIT with its NOTICE.md, its model is Apache-2.0 and downloaded separately, and Python already speaks to it over HTTP, so this port borrows no Crane code and needs no licence step, which answers D9's licence question. ffmpeg rather than a decoder crate: browsers send webm and Telegram ogg, both usually opus; whisper.cpp takes PCM; and the binary is already a Telegram voice-note dependency. Rejected: Python's engines through a worker op, because S3-DELETE removes them and Josh asked for a Rust engine and for Crane; Crane in process now, because crane-core brings candle, tokenizers and its model stack into gdaemon's build for a call the HTTP client already serves. The two traits keep each choice inside the crate.

### D111: Does gdaemon keep its crates.io publish? .github/workflows/release-gdaemon.yml:136-153 runs cargo publish -p gobby-daemon and its release job needs that job (:156), so a failed publish leaves no gdaemon release and no node bundles, and CP-NODE-CUT's update of the live node waits on that release. No plan or decision names this job.

**Default:** Drop it. S4-RELEASE removes the publish job and the release job's need on it, sets publish = false in crates/gdaemon/Cargo.toml, drops the pin at crates/gdaemon/tests/release_workflow.rs:15 and changes docs/guides/release-guide.md:13 to GitHub binaries. gdaemon ships through its GitHub release, which node install and D60's Homebrew resources read, and gclient, gcode, ghook, gmcp, gterm and gobby-core keep their crates.io publish. Alternative: keep the publish, which means publishing gobby-runtime, gobby-terminals, gobby-assets and every family crate, with a version on each path dependency and a crate-local copy of every embedded file, the template tree included, and tagging each of them ahead of gdaemon in every release.

**Rationale:** crates.io holds no gobby-daemon (checked 2026-11-04), and nothing in the repository installs it from there: Python's installer builds it from the checkout (src/gobby/cli/install_setup_gdaemon.py:84-92), and the job, its test pin and the release guide's row are the only places that name it with crates.io. The release guide already says a crate publishes only after its dependencies are indexed, so the alternative turns one release into a tag per family crate for a crate with no consumer. No other chunk removes the job, so without this decision the first gdaemon-v* tag stops at CP-NODE-CUT.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D115: Who gives the independent security approval on the port machine? land_commit and close_task both refuse a candidate whose paths match project_policy.security_paths, or whose task carries the security-review label, until a session holding approve_security_review that neither authored nor claimed the work has approved that exact SHA, unless the caller's definition holds waive_review_gates, which both rules test first since #25900 and which default's capabilities ['*'] hold (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-closure/security.yaml:25-60; src/gobby/workflows/agent_models.py:37-45; src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml:5; a landing evaluates with reason done, src/gobby/tasks/closure_snapshot.py:143, src/gobby/tasks/land_commit.py:639-646). Only the security-auditor definition names that capability (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/security-auditor.yaml:9); since #25900 default's capabilities ['*'] pass the approver check as well, though the rule still refuses an approval recorded by the caller, a claimant or a maker of the work. When this map was approved a spawned default-agent session could not spawn that definition, because its capabilities were not contained in the caller's; since #25900 default's ['*'] covers them, so spawn-capability-authority no longer refuses that spawn (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/tool-hygiene/spawn-capability-authority.yaml:5-26; src/gobby/workflows/condition_helpers_spawn.py:57-127; src/gobby/workflows/agent_models.py:37-51; src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml:5), and protocol step 7 has no auditor to send to unless Josh opens a seat on P.

**Default:** Josh opens the one static security-auditor seat on P before the first chunk claim, with gobby pipelines run support -i workspace=<workspace> -i project_path=<P's main checkout> -i seats=security-auditor -i report_to=<his own session ref> (docs/guides/runbooks.md:54-64), and it stays open through POST-CLEAN; a rerun of that command replaces the seat after a crash or a backup restore, because the runbook launches only a seat that is not live. A chunk or cutover session whose candidate those rules name, or whose brief names a security review, sends that seat the task ref and the full SHA and waits with wait_for_coordination until the seat records its security-review attestation on that SHA; each changed SHA needs its own approval. With no live seat a chunk session escalates its task to Josh, who reruns the command, and lands nothing, and once Josh says the seat is live it runs de_escalate_task and claim_task and resumes at the send. A cutover session instead reruns the command itself, because it is operator-opened, and asks Josh in its own terminal if that fails, and it keeps its claim either way, because escalate_task would release the claim that the landing freeze is bound to (its step 3). Any other candidate sends the seat nothing, because one seat that reviewed every candidate would queue the gated ones behind the rest, and each longer wait invites a moved tip and a new SHA to approve; Josh may widen that to every candidate in his answer, as his 2026-11-03 rule had it before the cut. A cutover does this for each land_commit once anything a linked commit lands matches, because the path scope covers every linked commit (src/gobby/tasks/land_commit.py:416-432). P's project_policy keeps security_paths unchanged, and nothing goes to A (D40 item 5). This takes no Python or template edit. If Josh declines the standing seat, D106's alternative applies. The seat starts on the provider project_policy.agent_model_defaults names, grok today, so that CLI must be on P (D119).

**Rationale:** With no approver on P, the first candidate that touches those paths stopped at land_commit with no way forward when this map was approved, and since #25900, when the security rules began to pass a default-definition caller (D115's question), it lands with no independent security review unless its session waits for one: SEAM-WS's crates/gdaemon/src/websocket/ modules; a pick of a fix in src/gobby/communications/; RULE-1A's prepared rewrite of the lookbehind patterns in src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/tool-hygiene/workflow-cli-authority.yaml:21 and src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/roles/seat-spawn-policy.yaml:67, in the cut that lands it, and the deactivation commit that reverts it; and S3-DELETE's deletion of the Python files on that list (.gobby/workflows/rules/project-operating-policy.yaml:35-265). R6, SEAM-CALLERS, A1-RUNTIME, SES-1 and SES-2 already name a security review as part of the chunk, and CP-RUNNER-REVIEW's packet lists its record, with no seat to produce it. A seat per candidate was rejected, because D39 lets chunk sessions be spawned and, when this map was approved, a spawned session could not launch this definition; since #25900 spawn-capability-authority, the rule that refused it, passes a spawned default-agent session (D115's question). Emptying security_paths on P was rejected, because it removes a protected check from the admission code that most needs it. A runs no auditor, and no chunk depends on cross-machine messaging (D40 item 5).

### D110: How does a hub-local native launch reach Python's terminal host until TERM-HOST? AG-1 goes live at CP-WAVE2-CUT and TERM-HOST at CP-WAVE5-CUT. TERM-1's spawn op on spawn_web_terminal (src/gobby/terminals/web_spawn.py:73-116) cannot carry an agent launch: it takes no agent_run_id, title, machine_id, auth_cli or theme source, cannot retry an existing pending row and takes no in-doubt claim (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_runtime.py:143-186; src/gobby/agents/spawn_placed.py:39-79, 204-242). AG-1's python_edits named no other op.

**Default:** One allow-listed worker op that AG-1 registers as its Rust caller, agent_runtime_spawn, with a small shim in R0's worker package over src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_runtime.py::_runtime_spawn (:54-140), Python's sole owner of the pending row, the prepare and the promote. The native launch path computes the provider command, environment and sandbox wrapping, and passes them with the project, machine, child session, parent session, agent run, title, auth_cli, working directory and timeout. The parent session is the theme source's input: _runtime_spawn reads it through spawner_theme_source (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_runtime.py:33-51), which gives no source for a spawner with no live native pane. The timeout fills SpawnRequest.timeout_seconds, because without it the prepare wait has no bound (src/gobby/agents/spawn_placed.py:278-282), and the op call's own timeout stays above it, so a slow spawn answers spawn_timeout and does not become an unknown outcome. No agent launch sets cancel_event today (src/gobby/agents/spawn_models.py:88; only web_spawn has its own, src/gobby/terminals/web_spawn.py:85), so the shim leaves it unset. The shim rebuilds SpawnRequest (src/gobby/agents/spawn_models.py:27-94) and ProviderSpawnPlan (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_providers.py:61-73), binds the worker's terminal manager and runtime registry (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py:91-106), and passes a SandboxLaunch that wraps nothing (src/gobby/agents/srt_runtime.py:113), because the native path has already wrapped the command. It passes no session manager and no run manager, so Python writes no session or agent_runs row (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py:243-247; src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_runtime.py:519); the native launch records them through AG-1's run-state store and gsessions' services, committing both rows before the op, because the pending row's foreign keys need them (crates/gcore/assets/schema/baseline.sql:5772, 5781), and it records the pid and terminal id from the result, which Python's update_runtime would have written. It returns SpawnResult's fields. A placed spawn has no Python binder to pass across the op, so AG-1 writes the pending row natively (crates/gterminals/src/repository.rs:84) with the columns create_pending sets (src/gobby/agents/spawn_placed.py:204-214: project, backend, ownership gobby, spawn key, machine, session, agent run and title), binds the pane through TERM-2's workspace operation, and passes that row's id as retry_terminal_id, which takes Python's in-doubt claim and generation bump (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_runtime.py:99-125; src/gobby/agents/spawn_placed.py:48-58, 197-200). Hub-local launches call it from CP-WAVE2-CUT until TERM-HOST's activation (CP-WAVE5-CUT), and S3-DELETE deletes it. A lost reply is an unknown outcome under R0's rule: the run reports spawn_in_doubt, and the launch is not retried blind. A refusal that returns before the attempt owns the row (a missing service, an unavailable host, terminal_in_doubt or retry_generation_cas_failed; src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_runtime.py:66-97; src/gobby/agents/spawn_placed.py:58-66, 223) leaves AG-1's own row pending, so AG-1 fails that row and compensates its placement. Past that point cleanup stays on the worker: _settle_native_spawn_failure fails the row and kills by spawn key (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py:109-133), and after a timeout _schedule_timeout_cleanup's done-callback or in-doubt owner settles it as a task on the op's event loop, after the op has returned (:330-392). None of them writes a session or agent_runs row, so AG-1 ends the run from the returned result and then calls deliver_completion (D71). Codex's deferred prompt sits outside _runtime_spawn (src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_codex.py:30-81), so the native launch schedules it after a successful op, through crates/gdaemon/src/nodes/agents/codex_prompt.rs and TERM-1's write op. Alternative: hub-local launches stay on Python's whole spawn path until CP-WAVE5-CUT.

**Rationale:** _runtime_spawn is the one place that owns a spawn's row, claim, prepare and promote, so the shim stays small. It does not fit D4's one-line clause because SpawnRequest and the plan are plain dataclasses that hold service objects only the worker has. Widening TERM-1's spawn_web_terminal op would change terminal_create's op for one caller. The alternative gives AG-1's hub store no live window until wave 5, the cut that also removes TERM-1's ops.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D116: Who starts the chunk sessions? Protocol step 1 runs in a default-agent session, 'spawned or operator-opened' (D39), and D23 caps how many run, but no text says who spawns them or applies the cap. A closing chunk session cannot start the next one for long, because each spawn deepens the chain and AGENTS.md rule 11 stops it at depth 5. A spawned session also claims its task at spawn (spawn_agent's claim defaults to true), before step 1's gport start could run from the main checkout. The coordinator seat that start gate item 10 lets Josh open on P is named only as a place to send a SHA. Nor can a spawned session do protocol step 8's push or CI read, because spawn_agent runs it under managed SRT.

**Default:** Josh opens one port dispatcher once start gate item 10's chunk tasks exist: a root, unspawned default-agent session in P's main checkout (AGENTS.md rule 15), in a pane the daemon started (start gate item 14), with the prompt 'Run the port dispatcher, D116 in docs/port/map-v2.md; spawn with model=<model> and reasoning_effort=<effort>', naming the model and effort its sessions run at. It claims no task and edits nothing. Its first turn sets session_lifecycle.stale_session_timeout_hours to 720 with gobby-config:patch_config_values, a live key, so that its row stays live through a review or a window with nothing to wake it. Each turn it calls list_ready_tasks(parent_task_id='#21542', limit=200), because the default limit of 10 can hide a ready checkpoint; skips an epic, because #21544 stays open as a container until R-RETIRE or COMMS-1 closes it (D31); and reads each other task once per context with get_task: its description's heading names its chunk and its Kind line gives its kind, both inside the preview of the offload envelope that a card over 15,000 characters comes back as, and that envelope shows labels only as a count (src/gobby/config/features.py:127-143; src/gobby/mcp_proxy/services/result_offload.py:300-315, 449), so it takes labels from list_tasks(parent_task_id='#21542', label=<label>, closed=False), one call for each of the three labels below. A claimed task is already running, so the dispatcher starts nothing on it, and it counts against D23's cap unless it is a review, a cutover or a chunk that a stop message holds, because a stopped session waits on fixes that need that room. That cap is 2 until CP-TEMPLATE-CUT closes, and after that the number Josh's go at CP-TEMPLATE-REVIEW or CP-WAVE3-REVIEW set, which he names when he prompts it after that close. A checkpoint review, a cutover and a task labelled needs-decision, needs-planning or clean-window are Josh's: the dispatcher names each in its final message. He answers a labelled task himself and opens the unspawned session a review or a cutover needs (start gate item 14), as he does for TMUX-PURGE, the first ready chunk (D129), and for GEN-ROLLBACK after it, unless his prompt says he refused its drill (D38). A review's session runs in the main checkout with no task worktree; it is unspawned because Josh answers it in that terminal and its no-go fixes and its resizing call create_task, which a spawned session is refused (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/task-enforcement/block-native-task-tools.yaml:5-19). For any other chunk, while the cap has room, it runs gport start <chunk id> from the main checkout, as cargo run --locked -q -p gobby-port -- start <chunk id> (by hand with get_task until P0-RECORD lands); a refusal starts nothing and goes in its final message. Then it calls spawn_agent(agent='default', task_id=<the task>, provider='claude', model=<model>, reasoning_effort=<effort>, checkout_mode='worktree', base_branch='0.9.0', prompt='Follow docs/port/protocol.md for chunk <chunk id> from step 2; your brief is this task's description.'). The three settings are required, because an unpinned spawn of default is refused (src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_launch_settings.py:97-106), and the provider is claude because protocol step 4's Workflow is a Claude Code tool and a headless provider cannot hold step 5's waits (src/gobby/agents/provider_capabilities.py:39-48; src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/headless_waits.py:34-68). When get_worktree_by_task finds a worktree left by a session that died, worktree_id replaces checkout_mode and base_branch and the prompt says to resume under step 1; a task whose replacement also dies goes in the final message for Josh instead of a third spawn. A task with no chunk heading is a task outside the map, which starts the same way with no gport start (protocol step 3), its prompt naming the task in place of a chunk. A spawned session cannot push or read CI, because spawn_agent runs it under managed SRT with no git network and no git or gh credentials (src/gobby/agents/sandbox_gate.py:22-33; docs/guides/sandboxing.md:245-249), and a test that launches an agent under SRT fails inside it (tests/e2e/conftest.py:490-498). So when one sends a landed SHA (protocol step 8), the dispatcher runs git push origin 0.9.0 from the main checkout in the background, because the pre-push hook's 1,800-second timeout (.gobby/project.json) outlasts a foreground tool call, then watches that commit's CI in the background so that its turn can end, and replies with the result, which is the hook's output when the pre-push hook refuses the push, or with superseded when a later push cancelled the run (D85); and when one sends a test command and its worktree path (protocol step 6), it runs that command bare in that worktree and replies with the result. A refusal that comes from web/node_modules behind web/package-lock.json is the dispatcher's own to fix, because no chunk session can: it runs npm ci in web and pushes again. A push that fails on the network, with GitHub down, is no result, so it sends no reply, and the session's resend after its next timeout retries it; a resend is safe, because the push then changes nothing and gh run list --commit <sha> finds the run. Several landed SHAs waiting take one push: the tip's run answers the tip, and each older SHA, which has no run of its own, gets superseded with the tip's SHA (D85). A run whose terminal reason is provider_error or provider_quota_exhausted (src/gobby/agents/watchdog/models.py:298-303), which is how a spawned session at a usage limit or in a provider outage usually ends, does not count toward the replacement limit above: the dispatcher spawns nothing more, names it in its final message, and lists again when Josh prompts it after the reset or the outage. A usage limit also holds this session's own pane on the provider's menu, where no wake lands (src/gobby/hooks/event_handlers/_misc.py:81-85, 415-416), so after a reset Josh submits a prompt in its pane first, and then in each review's and cut's. It runs nothing else a session sends. A red result is that session's to fix: when the found-work gate holds the dispatcher's stop over it (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/stop-gates/enforce-found-work-ladder.yaml:35-56), it edits nothing and stops again, which that rule's attempt limit lets through. When a session reports the test hub down, it starts it as start gate item 15 says. A spawned session cannot create a task, so a finding it must file (AGENTS.md rule 4, its last rung) reaches the dispatcher through send_message(target='parent'), and the dispatcher files it as a child of #21542 with that rung's label. The dispatcher starts no chunk that a stop message names until the release message arrives. Then it ends its turn: each session it spawned wakes it on completion (spawn_agent's notify_parent_on_completion), and Josh prompts it when TMUX-PURGE's or GEN-ROLLBACK's session, a review or a cut closes, a review's no-go files fixes or he closes a task he answered himself, as those sessions' final messages ask him to, because nothing else wakes it at those moments: a close outside its children sends it no wake. Since #25830 a global send or a project send with wake=true does wake an idle session with no claim whose composer is empty, the daemon's hold and DAEMON BACK notices among them (AGENTS.md rule 12; src/gobby/sessions/daemon_notices.py:39-68). During a daemon hold it starts nothing. Near the context limit it calls set_handoff(clear_session=false), never true, so that its session ref stays the one its children wake: a completion wake goes to the ref its subscriber row names and a terminal ref refuses it (src/gobby/events/wake.py:532-672; src/gobby/events/live_wake.py:110), and only the mailbox follows a clear successor (src/gobby/sessions/mailbox.py:470-485). That handoff carries its prompt's model and effort, the cap, any stop in force, each SHA or test command it owes a reply and each task whose replacement died, and it fetches its tools' schemas again afterwards. It holds no other state, so after a crash Josh opens a new one with the same prompt, naming any stop still in force and the current cap. It reads the task tree and sends one project send_message with wake=true naming its session ref, which a running session then uses in place of target='parent'; sessions the old one spawned cannot wake it, so Josh prompts it as they end. This takes no code or template edit. Alternative: Josh opens every chunk session himself, which D39 allows, and applies D23's cap by hand.

**Rationale:** The fleet stops at the first closed chunk without this: nothing else reads the ready list. One root session has the spawn authority that a spawned one may lack (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/tool-hygiene/spawn-capability-authority.yaml:5-26), never reaches the depth limit, and sits in the main checkout, where gport start runs. It needs no new seat definition, because the default agent already holds spawnable_agents ['*'] (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/agents/default.yaml). A chain of closing sessions was rejected for the depth limit. A coordinator seat was rejected because the port does not run runbook seats (rulings 3 and 11). Josh decides because it adds one session to the ones he opens. A spawned review session was rejected, because it cannot file the tasks its no-go and its resizing need, and routing them through the dispatcher adds a path for no gain when Josh answers a review in its terminal anyway. Giving spawned sessions a git credential was rejected, because a credential a managed run can read is open to every tool in it (docs/guides/sandboxing.md:245-249), and the bundled push rule's own text is to let the parent session push (src/gobby/install/shared/workflows/rules/worker-safety/no-push.yaml:6). Running a session's test command for it crosses the sandbox on purpose, for the few checks that prove nothing inside it, as a coordinator already does for a worker's process evidence (docs/guides/gterminal-development-guide.md:450-451). The setting is there because this session ends its turn with no timed wait, and a session with no activity for 24 hours is marked expired (src/gobby/storage/session_lifecycle.py, whose tmux exemption #25486's Decision 12 removes), even in a live pane. A wake to an expired session is declined (src/gobby/events/live_wake.py), a message to it answers sent_recipient_not_live, a reply wait on it returns owner_ended at once, and a day later Josh's prompt no longer revives the row (src/gobby/storage/sessions/_constants.py:100-106), so every session it spawned would stall at protocol step 6 or 8 with no sign to him. A liveness check still expires a session whose pane has gone (src/gobby/sessions/liveness_monitor.py), so the longer bound leaves only rows with a live pane in place. An hourly self-wake was rejected, because it spends a full uncached turn every hour for the whole port.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D117: What makes the port 1.0, and which chunk ships it? No chunk sets a 1.0 version: the gobby binary is the client at 0.1.135 at the cut (crates/gclient/Cargo.toml:3), and D55 only raises the tap formula's version_scheme above the Python releases. CP-NODE-CUT publishes the promoted versions, then POST-CLEAN bumps again, publishes nothing and closes #21542, so the port's last commit never ships. GitHub's default branch, main, still sits at 'Release v0.4.9' (45b0ea0e6a). CHANGELOG.md's [Unreleased] has no owner, because S3-DELETE keeps that file as a historical record. README.md:320 still promises a 0.6.0 Rust port release and README.md:352 calls Gobby pre-1.0, FEATURES.md:3 names the pre-port codebase and docs/guides/release-guide.md:8-17 lists versions from before the port. Installable today are PyPI 0.4.6, Homebrew 0.4.4 and standalone gcode 1.5.0 and ghook 0.7.2; v0.4.9 was tagged but never packaged.

**Default:** POST-CLEAN ships it, under CP-NODE-REVIEW's go (A16-POSTCLEAN). The product version is the gobby binary's: POST-CLEAN's activation commit sets crates/gclient/Cargo.toml's version to 1.0.0 in place of its patch bump, with Cargo.lock and the managed pins (V1A's version pins), and its tag is gclient-v1.0.0 (D55). Every other binary keeps its own version line and takes its ordinary bump. Any client change after the activation commit's promotion, an outcome-2 fix or a retry's, takes the next patch version (D39), and the release names that version. The same commit turns CHANGELOG.md's [Unreleased] into [1.0.0] with its date and adds the upgrade note: Python and the PyPI package are gone, and PyPI stays at 0.4.6 (D55); Homebrew's gobby formula now installs the Rust set (D60) and conflicts with gobby-code and gobby-hooks, so a Homebrew user uninstalls those first (D127); and nothing reads or migrates a 0.4.x install's SQLite hub (AGENTS.md rule 10), so it reinstalls from README.md's Install section and starts a fresh hub. It also makes README.md's 'What shipped in 0.4.x' (:155), 'Where it's going' (:298-349) and 'Status and contributing' (:352), FEATURES.md:3, ROADMAP.md's 'Where we are' (:12) and docs/guides/release-guide.md's Current Release Set (:8-17) state what ships. After the cut's clean window, and before it pushes its port/POST-CLEAN tag or clears the freeze, the hold and the pin, which it first extends to cover the publication and the live node's update, Josh fast-forwards origin/main to the commit the cut last promoted with git push origin <that SHA>:refs/heads/main: his admin role bypasses main's ruleset, which otherwise requires a pull request and the Merge Gate check, and a fast-forward keeps that commit at main's head. origin/main is an ancestor of the pre-cut branch today, so of 0.9.0, and CP-NODE-REVIEW's packet rechecks that with git merge-base --is-ancestor. He then pushes a release tag for each member whose manifest version differs from its last published tag, in the release guide's Tag Order, each tag in its own push, as at CP-NODE-CUT. The session checks each release as CP-NODE-CUT does, then that the client's release is marked latest, that crates.io holds gobby-client at that version and, once the tap's update job has run and Josh has merged the pull request it opens (D127), that GobbyAI/homebrew-tap's gobby formula names it and Tap CI's run on main passes, and sets the client release's notes from CHANGELOG.md's section with gh release edit --notes-file, because the workflow generates its own (.github/workflows/release-gclient.yml:196). The hub's release target then offers the new gdaemon to the live node through CP-NODE-CUT's path, and once gobby node status there reports healthy at that version, within the bound CP-NODE-CUT's window plan recorded, the session tags the last commit the cut promoted as port/POST-CLEAN, because an earlier tag would leave a release fix with no tag of its own (D40 item 4), pushes the tag, clears the freeze, the hold and the pin and closes the cut; a failure there is this cut's named trigger, under its trigger table. A release job that failed on a GitHub or crates.io outage is rerun with gh run rerun <id> --failed on the same tag, which is no outcome. Any other failed release job takes an outcome-2 fix (step 8) with the next patch version, a new release tag and main fast-forwarded to it, and a pushed tag never moves. Alternatives: publish CP-NODE-CUT's client tag as 1.0.0 and leave POST-CLEAN's drops to a 1.0.1, which calls a build 1.0 while it still carries the old USER.md paths and the tables POST-CLEAN drops; or give every binary 1.0.0, which moves gcode, at 1.9.118, below its own floor and below gobby-code 1.5.0, which crates.io already holds.

**Rationale:** Josh's word on #25279 is 'When we finish 1.0 there will be no python', and #21542 is titled Gobby 1.0 (ROADMAP.md:10), yet no text gives any binary that number or publishes the last commit. POST-CLEAN can own it with no new chunk: it is the last cut, it closes #21542, its go already carries public steps, and only its clean window proves the commit that gets the number. One number on the binary users run leaves each member's version line and release guard as they are. main has to move because it is what the repository shows and what a clone gets; it takes no merge, because it has no commit of its own since v0.4.9 (git rev-list HEAD..origin/main is empty). Josh decides because a version number, a tag and a default branch are public and irreversible.

### D120: The git guard's reference-transaction hook runs python3 for its policy read and its ghook payload (src/gobby/cli/installers/git_hooks.py:79-243). What runs those two steps at 1.0, when V1A drops Python from the dependency contract and the daemon runs no Python?

**Default:** V1B moves both snippets into ghook: one mode reads the committed allow_direct_integration_commits policy with its three exits, and one builds the payload from the branch and the staged paths and runs the same verdict mapping. The guard stays POSIX sh and calls only git and ghook. The new ghook ships in CP-PREDELETE-CUT's commit A (D39), commit A cites the install golden's changed lines as an accepted change (D84), and a guard installed earlier keeps python3 until the next gobby install. Alternative with no Rust: V1B writes the guard unchanged, V1A keeps python3 as a required dependency row, docs/guides/system-requirements.md keeps it, and S4-PROOF's python3 tripwire exempts git hooks.

**Rationale:** On a host with no python3 the guard refuses every update of the checked-out protected branch outside land_commit, with python3 is not on PATH, and a reference-transaction hook cannot be skipped with --no-verify, so a Python-free 1.0 install could make no permitted direct commit. The alternative keeps a Python dependency at 1.0, against V1A's dropped runtime row and S4-PROOF.

### D118: Who carries a cut when its session stops? From step 3 on, a cut session holds the task the landing freeze is bound to, the only copy of the hold token, which is in its transcript, and the observation window's watch. A dead pane, a usage limit or a provider outage stops it, and no text says who resumes it, where the token comes from or what happens if a trigger fires meanwhile. A rollback at CP-WAVE5-CUT can also take the pane that runs it, when the rehearsal measured that open panes do not survive the hand-back.

**Default:** Nothing rolls back by itself. A usage limit or a provider outage leaves the pane and the claim, because the session stays live (src/gobby/storage/tasks/_automation.py:89), and the background watch keeps logging. An outage's next timeout wake reaches it. A usage limit holds its pane on the provider's menu with automatic input blocked, so no wake lands until Josh submits a prompt there after the reset, and a session cannot release its own composer (src/gobby/hooks/event_handlers/_misc.py:81-85, 415-416; src/gobby/terminals/composer_ledger.py:667-676). Either way the session then reads the whole log since its last read and acts on any trigger it recorded. When the pane is gone, Josh opens a replacement, an unspawned default-agent session in a pane the daemon started (start gate item 14). It takes the claim with claim_task(force=true) once get_session shows the owner is no longer active, because the freeze admits the bound task's claim holder (GEN-ROLLBACK); reads the token, the pin and the commands from the task's window-plan attestations with list_attestations, the newest that names each; resumes at the step the task's attestations show; registers the watch and its waits again; and sets the hold again if it lapsed. It records any later change, a re-set hold's token included, as a new window-plan attestation with no supersedes_id, because only an attestation's author may correct it (src/gobby/tasks/attestations.py:299-306). If an outcome-3 trigger needs action while no session can answer, Josh runs step 9's gobby rollback restore from his own shell, and the replacement finishes the recipe. This takes no code or template edit. Alternative: a watchdog that restores the pinned generation on a health failure when no session answers, which needs new daemon code, cannot apply the trigger table Josh approved and could fire during a fix-forward.

**Rationale:** The hold and the freeze already outlive their session: a hold carries an expiry, Josh can clear it with gobby rollback hold clear, and the freeze binds to the task rather than to one session (GEN-ROLLBACK). So a second session can carry on from the task record once that record holds the token. Protocol step 1 already resumes a dead chunk session this way; a cut had no such text. The token guards against a promotion by mistake, not against a reader, and every session on P is Josh's, so an attestation is a fair place for it. Josh decides because this names him as the one who wakes for a dead cut session or one at its usage limit, and the live daemon can sit on an activated build until he does.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

Its fallbacks do not help at launch: only a dry run reads the chain (src/gobby/agents/dry_run.py:302). The seats then run as the policy names them, chunk sessions stay on Claude (D116), and a candidate's reviewer stays a different vendor from its author, as in the fleet that ran before the cut. No code, template or policy edit. Alternative: P's policy commit (start gate item 12) sets both agent_model_defaults entries to provider claude with a Claude model, so step 7's call and D115's command stay as written, which needs no new CLI or login but loses a second vendor's eye on every landing.

**Rationale:** Without one of the two, the first spawn of step 7's seat and D115's command fail on P, and the first landing has no reviewer. Provider arguments on each spawn were rejected for the alternative, because every session and D115's command would then carry a model literal that one policy entry already decides. Josh decides because this adds credentials to a second machine and spends on two more vendors, or trades away a different vendor's review.

### D124: Differential replay never sends an attack, so a Python trust check that no brief names passes every oracle when a port leaves it out, and reqwest and axum defaults differ silently from httpx and Starlette. What binds a session porting a module that holds such a check, what do its native HTTP clients, body reads and child processes default to, and who renders the bundled prompts that delimit untrusted text before the rules crate exists?

**Default:** (1) A chunk that ports a Python module ports every check in it that refuses, bounds, redacts or confines untrusted input, named in its brief or not, and replays the Python negative tests that pin the check as native cases. A check it cannot carry is a Found Work gap, never a silent difference. (2) A native HTTP client keeps the policy of the Python client it replaces. httpx follows no redirect unless that client sets follow_redirects=True and reqwest follows ten by default, so each client states redirect::Policy::none() or Python's rule, keeping Python's same-origin rule where it has one (src/gobby/mcp_proxy/transports/http.py:25-40). trust_env=False becomes .no_proxy(), as crates/gcore/src/loopback.rs:137 does. (3) A native route that reads a body states its limit, because axum's Bytes, Json, String and Multipart extractors stop at DefaultBodyLimit's 2 MiB and Starlette has no limit: Python's limit where it has one, otherwise one at least as large as the largest recorded use. (4) PLANS adds a subset render with the untrusted filter to crates/gruntime/src/prompts.rs, on the refusal that .gobby/plans/memory-family.md Decision 12 and SES-3B already set, so no template engine enters outside the rules crate (D76), and MUX-2 reuses it. Hook prompts render through RULE-1B's engine, which HOOK-2 reaches. chunk_protocol's note carries (1) to (3), (5) and (6). Alternative for (4): minijinja in the plans crate too, which renders any override and takes D76's exception and an earlier first consumer for the pin. (5) A native child whose Python spawn passes env= starts with env_clear() and that map, because Python's env= replaces the environment and Rust's Command inherits gdaemon's, secrets included. (6) A chunk that reuses existing native code lists Python's steps on that path against the native code's before its oracle runs, and ports each missing step or reports it under Found Work, because an oracle that compares native output with native output proves nothing about parity. AG-1's launch path lacked four of Python's steps, found only because one review compared them; the other reused paths have not been compared.

**Rationale:** A corpus records real traffic, so an absent signature check, Host check, redaction or body cap replays equal. The guards found without a chunk's words include /mcp's Host and Origin policy, the login limiter, Argon2id, the sign-in link, the webhook SSRF transport, comms verification and access, the terminal output redactor, the prompt delimiter and the pipeline environment filter; each now has an owner, and the rule covers what that sweep did not sample. No crate in the workspace sets a redirect policy, and the Python clients take both values (src/gobby/ai/decisions_wire.py:320; src/gobby/communications/attachments.py:47). PLANS lands before RULE-1B and RULE-1B reaches PLANS, so PLANS cannot call the rules crate, and expansion/user's six | untrusted expressions must render with their delimiter (tests/prompts/test_untrusted_content_prompts.py:56). A port that only copies an env= map keeps gdaemon's environment in the child, which undoes a deliberate minimal one (src/gobby/adapters/acp_terminal.py:50-69, 148; src/gobby/agents/srt_monitor.py:331), as crates/gdaemon/src/nodes/agents/sandbox/monitor.rs:204 already avoids.

**Resolved by:** none recorded

### D125: The guard ports need crates that Cargo.lock lacks: argon2 for AUTH's password hash, lettre and async-imap for COMMS-2's email adapter, and reqwest's socks feature, which adds tokio-socks, for its Telegram proxy. Teams' RS256 check could add jsonwebtoken. The cron, backup and skills plans also name croner with chrono-tz, tar and zip, which no map decision records, and SKILLS and AG-1 need tar before the backup chunk does. May they enter, and at which pins?

**Default:** Yes. Each is exact-pinned at its newest release that is at least two weeks old when its first consumer starts (AUTH for argon2, COMMS-2 for lettre, async-imap and the socks feature, CRON for croner and chrono-tz, SKILLS for zip and tar, with AG-1's Zig mirror and V1A taking tar after it), only in the crate that needs it, as D8 and D76 place theirs. argon2 stays out of gclient, gcode and ghook. Teams' RS256 and Discord's Ed25519 use ring directly, which Cargo.lock already holds at 0.17.14, with the check's alg fixed at RS256, so jsonwebtoken does not enter. The cron plan's croner 4.0.1 yields to this pin rule, and the Skills plan's zip, its single authorized library, gains tar, because the GitHub topic hub unpacks the tarball it downloads. Alternatives: jsonwebtoken 11.1.0 for Teams, which also needs a crypto backend choice; moving email after 1.0 instead of taking its two crates, which reverses D28's list.

**Rationale:** crates.io on 2026-11-05. argon2 0.6.0, 2026-08-27, MIT OR Apache-2.0, msrv 1.85, owners newpavlov and github:rustcrypto:kdfs, 71.8M downloads. lettre 0.11.23, 2026-08-03, MIT, msrv 1.85, owners amousset and github:lettre:release, 23.1M. async-imap 0.12.0, 2026-09-30, MIT OR Apache-2.0, msrv 1.88, owners dignifiedquire and link2xt, 2.16M; the older imap crate is synchronous and its last release, 3.0.0-alpha.15, dates from 2025-02-08, so it is rejected. tokio-socks 0.5.3, 2026-05-29, MIT, owner sticnarf, 24.9M. jsonwebtoken 11.1.0, 2026-09-16, MIT, msrv 1.88, owner Keats, 234M. croner 4.0.3, 2026-11-04, then 4.0.2, 2026-10-21, and 4.0.1, 2026-10-02, MIT, owner Hexagon, 12.2M, so the pin is 4.0.2 or newer by CRON's start. chrono-tz 0.10.4, 2025-07-11, MIT OR Apache-2.0, msrv 1.65, owners quodlibetor and github:chronotope:release-manager, 190.4M, which the cron plan names beside croner. tar 0.4.46, 2026-05-18, MIT OR Apache-2.0, msrv 1.63, owner cgwalters, 278.6M. zip 9.3.0, 2026-11-03, MIT, msrv 1.88, owners Plecra and Pr0methean, 331.7M, with three releases in the last three days, which the pin rule already waits out. All clear the workspace's 1.88 floor (D76). Stored password rows hold Argon2id hashes (src/gobby/identity.py), so the crate is not optional, and ring already provides both signature checks, so a JWT crate adds a dependency tree for one fixed algorithm. SKILLS needs tar first because Python's topic hub downloads /tarball/<sha> and unpacks it (src/gobby/skills/hubs/github_topic.py:128, 476), and AG-1's Zig mirror unpacks tarball entries (src/gobby/agents/zig_packages.py:44-57). ring is the workspace's one crypto backend (crates/gdaemon/Cargo.toml:40).

### D127: Who writes GobbyAI/homebrew-tap's formula change? D55 and D60 say the tap makes it before the first gclient-v* tag after commit B, and CP-NODE-REVIEW had Josh confirm it is merged, but no chunk or person is named to write it, and a spawned session cannot reach github.com.

**Default:** CP-NODE-REVIEW's session opens the pull request on GobbyAI/homebrew-tap while it builds its packet, through gh api and gh pr create with no local clone, because it is unspawned and holds P's git and gh logins. The pull request makes D55's and D60's changes: the gobby formula installs the client archive with its version_scheme raised and carries the member binaries as D60 decides, and the tap's update job waits until every member's archive at those versions exists. The change goes in the tap's .github/scripts/update_formulae.py as well as the formula, because the update job rewrites Formula/gobby.rb from that script's template on every dispatch, and the job loses its brew update-python-resources step, which only a Python formula can take. The template's test block changes with it: it asserts 'gobby, version <version>' from gobby --version and runs --version on gcode, ghook, gloc and gsqz, so it would fail on the client that CP-NODE-CUT's tag releases. It asserts the version alone, as the script's helper template already does, which also holds through POST-CLEAN's rename, and its loop names the binaries the formula provides. Tap CI's Homebrew install step takes the flags the native install verb takes. The script's four helpers go with their formulae and depends_on lines, gobby-local and gobby-squeeze having already left this workspace (crates/CHANGELOG.md:723), and the gobby formula declares conflicts_with for gobby-code and gobby-hooks, because each links the same bin/gcode or bin/ghook. The packet names the pull request, and Josh merges it before CP-NODE-CUT's gclient tag, the first after commit B, which dispatches to it. The update job ends by opening a pull request of its own and merges nothing, so after each gclient tag Josh merges that one too; until he does, the formula does not name the release. A request the job opens with the default token starts no run of its own, so Tap CI's run on main after the merge is the check. Nothing in this repository changes. Alternative: Josh writes and merges the change himself, and the packet only reminds him.

**Rationale:** The tap's formula change is a deliverable that CP-NODE-CUT's tag and POST-CLEAN's release check both need, and it had no author, so the first reader to notice would have been the review that needs it merged. No earlier chunk can write it: S3-NAME and S4-RELEASE run spawned, with no route to GitHub (D116), so neither can read the tap's formula or its update job. A new chunk would add a task for one pull request in another repository, with no oracle in this one. Josh decides because the session writes to a repository outside this one under his login.

**Default:** The port moves only between a cutover's close and the next chunk claim: no chunk session, review or cutover open, every landed commit pushed, and no hold or freeze set. That day P takes a verified hub backup, and the Linux machine is seeded from it by start gate item 17's steps, with its main checkout on origin's 0.9.0: the base install, the restore, P's KEK, its own machine id, the second install run, the checkout binding, the store rebuilds and the web build. After the move the laptop lands nothing on 0.9.0, so one install at a time is P. A move between cuts leaves no window, hold, freeze or open worktree to carry across, and the seeding steps are the ones item 17 already uses for A. The generation store is install-local, so the first promotion rebuilds it.

### D129: Where does the tmux purge (#25486) run, now that it did not finish on 0.5.0 before the cut?

**Default:** TMUX-PURGE is the first chunk on 0.9.0, and GEN-ROLLBACK depends on it, so ruling 8's outcome holds: every other chunk starts from a tree with no tmux. Its two migrations land outside a cut and are destructive, where D22 keeps schema steps inside cuts and expand-only. Its Python edits are live, outside D4's dormant list. Putting it first on 0.9.0 is the closest order left, and it keeps true what the chunks already assume about a tree with no tmux (R4, AG-3, MCP-CRATE, RULE-1B, TERM-HOST, V1B). Retention would not help the leaves that matter most: gobby rollback restore refuses across a schema identity change, and the purge's migrations change it.

### D130: Where does #25935, removing /clear handoffs, run?

**Default:** Josh approved #25935 for implementation on 0.9.0 before REC-SESSIONS-AGENTS records handoff cases. set_handoff is compact-only and rejects both obsolete clear_session selector values. After task closure, continue in place or start a fresh default session with /clear or /new; fresh sessions inherit no handoff, claims, persona, workflow, parent, or run lineage. Never send /clear to a spawned CLI; if received, cancel and fully tear it down with claim recovery and parent notification. Legacy pending clear attempts are disarmed without replay or new receipts; historical authored content and receipts remain readable, with expand-only storage. REC-SESSIONS-AGENTS records compact and fresh-session/legacy-retirement cases; SES-2 loses the clear gate and lineage transfer; CHAT-2 loses clear handoff delivery; HOOK-2 and D75 lose clear dispatch and continuation; CP-WAVE5-CUT loses clear ladder triggers. Python removal lands before the recorder, so obsolete behavior is never recorded or ported. No spawned CLI survival or credential cutover is required. The task closes before POST-CLEAN.

**Rationale:** Josh, 2026-11-12: 'File a task for removing /clear handoffs after we get the move done and are working on the port.' Then: 'add it to the port work'. He named the task and its timing and no design, so the map records where it sits and leaves the design to its plan.

## Rulings

### Operator decisions for the 1.0 chunk map (2026-11-03, Josh, session gobby#19401)

Source: AskUserQuestion answers and chat messages in session gobby#19401.

1. No gobby-plan and no plan mode. The deliverable is a chunk map that ultracode can execute:
   each chunk is one Gobby task whose description is a self-contained brief.
2. Order: migrate away from the Python daemon first (runner-first; Rust owns the daemon
   process), so route families become libraries of the Rust daemon. Hub/node stays in scope.
3. Chunk sessions run locally with the hub, as default Gobby agents (the `default`
   definition), so runbook policies do not restrict them while they work.
4. Port branch: Josh will "probably start branch 0.9.0 when all the friction work is done";
   Claude creates a worktree off 0.9.0. Chunks land on 0.9.0, not 0.5.0.
5. One approval for the whole chunk map. Each chunk's workflow runs its own
   debt-not-to-port pass and adversarial design and code review (replaces the 10-24
   per-family approval stop for this port).
6. Before the port starts: wrap up #24238 (decision classifier adapter), #23591 (provider
   quota and usage ledger), #24821 (terminal and client), #24820 (code index), and the
   current worktrees (22 open on 2026-11-03). After the start, Python changes are limited
   to bug fixes and friction work.
7. Tests are rewritten from contracts, not translated (heavy test debt).
8. The tmux purge (#25486) is happening now on 0.5.0. It belongs to the start gate, so no
   0.9.0 chunk carries tmux.
9. R7 and other transitional mechanism: "I just want to get this done/away from python as
   efficiently and correctly as possible." Keep transitional machinery only where it is
   the cheapest way to keep correctness evidence at each chunk boundary.
10. Coordinator gobby#19309 relayed Josh's ruling (2026-11-03): no new tasks or scope
    additions until the friction queue is done. The chunk map is designed now, read-only;
    creating chunk tasks waits for Josh's map approval and his word on that ruling.

11. Cutover ownership (Josh, 2026-11-03 ~18:00 CT): "we're not building this with runbook
    agents, so a default agent (like you) would handle it". The cutover and any promotion are
    chunk work for a default-agent session, not the Release Manager seat (memory 928706b3 is
    about the runbook fleet).
12. Comms (Josh): "I don't know if those work. The goal is to have a unified gobby-comms
    interface that works with Telegram, Slack, Teams, email, SMS, iMessage, etc. Not sure what
    gobby_chat is or how it's used currently. Might be legacy code." The Rust target is one
    adapter contract for every channel. Whether each Python adapter works is unknown, so each
    one is verified before it can serve as an oracle.
13. Discord (Josh): "Discord if we can make it work, otherwise defer to after 1.0."
14. Deletion, checkpoints and rollback (Josh): "Yes, it can be deleted in one go, or we can
    strangler fig it. Setting checkpoints during development is probably a good idea, especially
    after we port the daemon runner, the mcp proxy, and the first family. My idea was that each
    route family would be its own rust binary or library, with gcore being the main library.
    Take gtask for example: Suppose we make an update to gtask that breaks it. We should have the
    previous version as a backup binary that we can roll back to and fix if needed."
    - FINAL (Josh, ~18:35-18:45 CT, three messages): "By checkpoints I mean we pause and check
      that we're going in the right direction. Once we build one route family that will serve as
      the template for others, for example." Then: "Checkpoints are review pauses, then cutover
      as you described above." Then: "Not a single end-of-port cutover."
      - Each checkpoint is a review pause (a go/no-go direction check with Josh) followed by a
        live cutover of what that stage built: promote binaries built from 0.9.0 (generation
        kept), flip the stage's runner or families to native, restart outside quiet hours with a
        global announcement, a bounded live observation window with named rollback triggers,
        and a rehearsed rollback.
      - Named checkpoints: after the daemon runner, after the MCP proxy, and after the first
        route family (the template for the rest); later ones per wave as the map proposes.
      - Settled item 15 and the R7 row are revised: promotion happens at checkpoint cutovers.
        There is no merge into 0.5.0 (ruling 15).
      - Python deletion: in one go at the end, or strangler; both are acceptable.
    - Per-family rollback (the gtask example) is an architecture requirement for the cutover and
      later. Proposed D36: families are library crates on gcore inside one gdaemon; promotion
      keeps the previous binary generation; a rollback verb restores it. Neither exists today
      (bin_set_coherence.py:88-153 replaces in place). Per-family processes rejected for 1.0:
      hot paths would cross IPC, and single-owner state would need a distributed owner. Awaiting
      Josh at map approval.
15. Branching (Josh, ~18:36 CT): "we're not merging back into 0.5.0. 0.9.0 will become the new
    working branch we'll merge into, and you'll be working in a worktree like 0.9.0-rust".
    - 0.9.0 becomes the integration branch: project_policy's integration branch moves, and the
      main checkout sits on 0.9.0.
    - Port sessions work in worktrees off 0.9.0 (named like 0.9.0-rust) and land each chunk into
      0.9.0 with land_commit. Close gates and blocked_by readiness work unchanged.
    - D1 is resolved with no code change, so options A and B and the start-gate item drop. The
      0.5.0 merge-forward step drops too.
    - The consequence is lost isolation: the live Python daemon runs from the 0.9.0 main checkout,
      so every port chunk must keep it working. That means no template moves or edits until
      retirement, and port migrations or schema inputs must not break the restart and cutover
      preflight (schema identity, `gdaemon schema plan`, installed set); verify how that
      preflight treats checkout schema inputs newer than the installed gdaemon. CI triggers
      must cover 0.9.0 (ci.yml:3-5, rust-ci.yml), and Python edits stay inert under D4.

16. The runner is a firm switchover (Josh, 2026-11-03 ~19:15 CT, two messages): "I think the
    runner needs to be a firm switchover. Either it works, it works enough that we can debug it,
    or we roll back to python and reassess." Then: "When we move the runner off python I want to
    be off the python runner for the remainder of the build."
    - CP-RUNNER's cutover is one-way. Its window ends in one of three outcomes:
      - it works;
      - it works well enough to debug, so the session stays on the Rust runner and fixes forward;
      - or it rolls back to the Python runner (the pinned pre-cutover generation), and Josh
        reassesses before any retry.
    - The runner gets no dual mode and no runtime runner switch. Ruling 9 stands for the runner,
      and R1 stays reduced. Before CP-RUNNER, R0-R6 land dormant through code defaults, and
      CP-RUNNER-CUT's own commit activates the Rust runner.
    - Once the window closes with the first or second outcome, the Python runner is retired for
      the rest of the build. Its launch path is removed, and every later rollback target is a
      Rust-runner generation. Python domain services keep running as a worker under the Rust
      runner until their families port.
    - Consequence: CP-RUNNER must cover the whole runner before CP-MCP and the first family,
      because nothing can fall back to the Python runner afterward. The whole runner is R0-R6:
      startup, readiness, recovery, periodic work, service lifecycle, shutdown and restart.
      Dependencies that put R2-R6 after A1 or Stage 2 are moved; for example, R4's native
      rotate_due registration moves into A1-RUNTIME.
    - During fix-forward, the rollback target is the pinned pre-cutover generation, never only
      the previous generation, because fix-forward promotions would push the previous one out.
    - Claude's default, not part of the ruling: apply the same three-outcome window and pinned
      target to every checkpoint cutover. Prefer code-default dormancy plus generation rollback
      over runtime switches everywhere; this is the least mechanism, and it matches Josh's gtask
      example ("the previous version as a backup binary").

17. Database backup at every Rust cutover (Josh, 2026-11-03 ~19:20 CT): "each rust cutover should
    include a database backup, in case we're making changes that can't be easily rolled back (like
    removing a lot of dead tables/columns from the database found as tech debt during the port)".
    - Every cutover chunk takes a verified backup of the hub database, plus any other datastore its
      changes touch. The backup comes before any migration or activation, and the rehearsal proves
      the restore.
    - It also allows destructive schema cleanup during the port. Dead tables and columns found as
      port tech debt can be dropped at a cutover instead of waiting for a post-port cleanup.
    - Claude's reading, not Josh's words: "dead" means that nothing reads or writes the table or
      column in the new generation, in the pinned rollback generation, or in Python while Python
      exists. A drop like that keeps the generation rollback valid.
    - A change that the pinned generation cannot run on makes the backup restore the rollback, and
      that restore loses every write made after the backup. The cutover names such a change as an
      irreversible step, and Josh gives his go for it in the review.
    - D22 changes from "expand-only until after the final window" to:
      - expand-only by default;
      - drops of verified-dead tables and columns allowed at a cutover, under its backup;
      - any other destructive change named as an irreversible step in the review packet.

- CONFIRMED (Josh, ~19:25 CT, "I agree with those"). These are now rulings, not Claude's
  defaults:
  - every checkpoint cutover gets the three-outcome window and a pinned pre-cutover generation;
  - dormancy works through code defaults plus generation rollback, with no runtime switches;
  - ruling 17's reading: "dead" means unused in the new generation, the pinned generation and
    Python; any other destructive change is an irreversible step that needs Josh's go at its
    checkpoint review.

- Operator direction on friction #25639 (Josh, ~19:55 CT): "I talked to 19309 about your friction.
  We're working on it. Trying to make default agents fairly unrestricted and handoff should never
  apply to subagents."
  - Josh and coordinator gobby#19309 own #25639. This session does not work on it.
  - For the map: chunk sessions are default agents (ruling 3), so the protocol preamble states
    policy only (for example, gobby-managed worktrees off 0.9.0, land_commit, the code-reviewer
    seat). It must not encode workarounds for default-agent restrictions, because those
    restrictions are being removed.
  - The "no set_handoff while workflow agents run" rule holds only until #25639 lands. The doc
    names #25639 as a start-gate dependency and does not put the workaround in the briefs.

18. Port machine (Josh, 2026-11-03 ~23:20 CT, direction): "I think when the agents done with all
    these fixes to runbooks etc., I'm going to back up everything and move us to a separate machine
    so this can run without game-goblins. I'll be running two independent gobby installs. I'll
    figure out a way to have the agents communicate over tailscale."
    Consequences Claude raised with Josh (open until he answers; fold into map-v2 after the fix
    agent, as start-gate items plus a topology decision):
    - A restore of everything gives the port machine A's comms bot tokens, webhooks, cron jobs and
      external MCP OAuth (rotating refresh tokens). Scrub or disable them before its first start;
      the comms chunks use test channels there (generalizes lens finding COEX-M3).
    - Identity: the port machine gets a fresh machine id, and its task and session numbering is
      offset after the restore, so a ref such as #25650 or gobby#19309 never names two things.
    - Branch on machine A after the cut: does 0.5.0 freeze (ruling 15), or keep landing fixes that
      must be forward-ported to 0.9.0? Claude's default: freeze, except fixes A needs to keep
      running, each cherry-picked to 0.9.0.
    - Cross-machine agent messaging is hub-node scope (NODEHUB, S4-NODE); any tailscale link built
      before them is a stopgap the port replaces.
    Josh's answer (~23:30 CT): "yes, I'm talking about a stopgap. I'll fork 0.9.0 here under a
    different branch, like 0.9.0-gg. If I have to make any gobby changes here I'll push them to this
    remote branch and you can cherry pick."
    So: the port runs on the new machine on 0.9.0; this machine forks 0.9.0 as 0.9.0-gg at the cut
    (0.5.0 freezes); this machine's gobby fixes are pushed to origin/0.9.0-gg; the port
    cherry-picks them into 0.9.0. Claude's proposed mechanics for the map: cherry-pick with -x;
    every checkpoint review lists pending ones with `git cherry 0.9.0 origin/0.9.0-gg` and picks
    them before its cutover (and on Josh's request in between); a fix to Python that a chunk has
    already ported goes to the owning chunk to port or drop; 0.9.0-gg's own commit that points its
    project_policy integration branch at 0.9.0-gg is never cherry-picked.
    Josh, same exchange: "but that way I can start upgrading gobby on the other machine as your
    fixes go live". Claude's reading (stated back to Josh for correction): the flow is two-way.
    This machine (0.9.0-gg) takes each checkpoint once the port machine closes it with outcome 1.
    Map consequences: an outcome-1 CP-*-CUT tags its activated commit on 0.9.0; every cutover
    recipe is written to replay on any install (its own verified backup, the same migrations,
    promotion and activation); this machine adopts by merging the tag into 0.9.0-gg and replaying
    the recipe. Concern 1 (rollback vs migrations) applies to that replay too.
    Josh: "problem is my commits will collide with gobby tasks on the porting machine". Claude's
    fix (proposed): the auto-linker scans the whole message for [project-#N], project-#N and
    fixes/closes/refs project-#N, validating the project name (src/gobby/tasks/commits.py:578-585),
    and new task and session numbers are MAX(seq_num)+1 per project (storage/tasks/_creation.py:175,
    storage/sessions/_crud.py:262). So, once after the restore and before the port machine creates
    any task or session, an operator SQL update gives one row seq_num 99999 in each table; the port
    machine then numbers from #100000 and no ref from either machine names a task on the other, in
    either direction, merges included. The cherry-pick step also puts the port task's own ref in
    the subject and rewrites this machine's refs to gobby-gg-#N, which the linker skips.
    Josh (~23:45 CT): "why wouldn't I do the offset on the non-port (this machine)? that way we keep
    git continuity on the gobby commits and don't have a massive jump in task numbers?" Claude
    agreed: SETTLED DIRECTION, offset THIS machine (0.9.0-gg), so the port line that becomes 1.0
    keeps natural numbering. Condition: the offset (one row to seq_num 99999 in tasks and in
    sessions, here; SUPERSEDED by item 21: 999999 on placeholder rows, after the backup) runs in
    the same daemon-stop window as the backup that seeds the port machine,
    so nothing is numbered here in between. Cherry-picks still put the port task's ref in the
    subject; this machine's refs (#100000+) can never resolve on the port machine. Disjoint ranges
    also let this machine's tasks be imported into the port DB later.
19. Rollback vs migrations (Josh, 2026-11-03 ~23:57 CT, on concern 1): "We should probably figure
    out a workaround". DIRECTION: build one. Claude's proposal, to fold into map-v2 as a D22
    rewrite plus a small chunk that ships at the first cutover (Josh approves it with the map):
    - Facts checked in code: schema_migrations stores (version, filename, checksum)
      (crates/gcore/src/schema/runner.rs:329; resolve_pending_migrations in runner_plan.rs).
      Destructive migrations already carry the `-- gobby:destructive` directive
      (runner_plan.rs:281). An older binary refuses a newer hub in at least three places:
      gdaemon's start check (crates/gdaemon/src/main.rs:286-291), validate_lineage
      (runner_plan.rs:153-169) and resolve_pending_migrations ("receipt v{version} has no matching
      embedded schema asset"). The chunk inventories every other gate that compares a hub head
      with a binary or pin, the Python restart and cutover preflight included.
    - Expand window: a destructive migration's filename carries a marker as well as the
      directive, and a test enforces the pairing both ways. Every gate accepts a hub head newer
      than the code head when each receipt above the code head has a non-destructive filename and
      each receipt at or below it matches the embedded assets as today. A destructive receipt
      above the head still refuses and is named. No new column is needed.
    - Bootstrap: the tolerance carries no schema step of its own and ships at the first cutover
      (CP-RUNNER-CUT), so its rollback target never meets a newer hub. From then on every retained
      generation tolerates expand steps, so rollback across one is binary-only (plus the
      activation revert of COEX-B1). Drops stay at CP-FINAL-CUT and POST-CLEAN with Josh's go,
      where rollback is that cut's backup restore. The same holds when 0.9.0-gg replays a
      checkpoint.
20. Binary promotion (Josh, ~23:57 CT, on concern 2): "promote binaries on checkpoints right? we
    don't want to run a full rust recompile every time a binary is updated". RULED: promote only
    at checkpoint cutovers (ruling 14 stands). Consequences for map-v2: chunk sessions may be
    spawned agents (start_gate[13] goes); the fix agent's separate per-landing schema steps
    (D22, start_gate[9], the standing go for seven expand steps) fold into the checkpoint
    cutovers: a schema-carrying chunk lands in its checkpoint's cut, before activation and under
    that cut's backup, never between checkpoints and never in a post-activation window.
21. Project rename (Josh, ~23:55 CT): "Should we rename the project here too so commits tasks can
    never collide?" Claude's answer: no. The offset (item 18) already keeps new numbers disjoint
    in both directions; every task numbered before the backup is the same task on both machines,
    so a ref to one linking on both is correct. Rename costs: .gobby/project.json is tracked (name "gobby"),
    so the rename is a 0.9.0-gg commit every cherry-pick must skip and every adoption merge may
    conflict on; AGENTS.md and docs say `[gobby-#NNNNN]`. `gobby projects rename` exists
    (src/gobby/cli/projects.py:281). The port machine needs about 74,000 more tasks to reach
    #100000; for more margin, start this machine at #1000000 with the same one-row step.
    Josh (~00:05 CT 2026-11-04): "we've accumulated 25K over the last year, so 1000000 is probably
    correct". SETTLED: no rename; this machine numbers tasks and sessions from 1000000 (this
    supersedes item 18's 99999). Start-gate recipe, operator only, this machine only:
    1. While the daemon runs, create one placeholder task ("numbering offset marker", closed)
       and open and end one throwaway session. Never re-number an existing row: that changes a
       real task's or session's ref.
    2. Stop the daemon and take the backup that seeds the port machine. The placeholders travel
       with their natural numbers, which is harmless there.
    3. After the backup, set the two placeholders' seq_num to 999999 here. Never before the
       backup: the port machine would inherit the offset.
    4. Start the daemon and confirm MAX(seq_num) is 999999 in both tables for the project.
    Prerequisite on 0.5.0, before step 3 and before the fork so 0.9.0 and 0.9.0-gg both carry
    it (Claude's scan of src, crates and web/src for digit-capped regexes found exactly these):
    - src/gobby/memory/dream/related.py:52 `#(\d{3,6})`, used at :787 to pull task refs from
      memory text: a seven-digit ref is read as its first six digits, which names another task.
    - src/gobby/voice/text_normalizer.py:28 `#(?=\d{1,5}(?!\d))`: refs of six or more digits
      skip the spoken-number rewrite.
    MET (landed) 2026-11-04: #25649 at 82b3f2806d on 0.5.0, closed 00:37 CT by gobby#19309; its
    sweep found no other capped pattern. Live on A from its next daemon start. Recipe additions
    (parent, from code): the placeholder task is a root task and step 3 sets path_cache to
    '999999' with its seq_num; six MAX(seq_num)+1 sites, over tasks and sessions only.

25. CI does not gate the port (Josh, 2026-11-13 ~14:00 CT, in session gobby#19401's own pane). On
