    want to avoid sidequests." Then, to the proposal that CI on 0.9.0 be advisory and a chunk's gate
    be its own local validation: "agreed, we don't worry about CI until we get near the end of the
    port when we're validating it". Then: "do we have a capture of what failed at CI so the porting
    agents can discern whether it's a bad test or bad behavior?" That capture is
    docs/port/ci-baseline-at-cut.md.
    The map author's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. Until the port's final
    validation, a red CI run on 0.9.0 blocks no landing, no close and no cutover, and nobody fixes
    CI as a task of its own. This overrides the map and the protocol wherever they make CI a
    condition: start gate item 9's 'both workflows treated as required' and item 11's 'fixed on
    0.9.0 before the first chunk lands', D34's default, D85, protocol step 8's CI check and each
    cutover's refusal to activate a commit whose CI is red. Their text is not rewritten. A chunk's
    gate is protocol step 6's local validation. A failure listed in the baseline is old; a chunk
    that works in the code behind a listed failure fixes it as found work, and a failure that is
    not listed and that the chunk caused is that chunk's to fix. Josh says when the final
    validation begins.
    Note, 2026-11-15 (a fact, not a ruling). Task #26070 fixed the inherited failures and CI is
    green on 0.9.0 at 050479408a; docs/port/ci-baseline-at-cut.md opens with the runs. Josh pasted

    No listed failure remains, so a red run after 050479408a is judged under the ruling's last
    case, a failure that is not listed.
26. A conversation resumed during a daemon outage (Josh, 2026-11-16 ~08:15 CT, in session
    gobby#19401's own pane). Task #26074 asked him to choose between leaving the behaviour and
    admitting the queued start, and he first agreed to leave it: "your recommendation is sound".
    Then: "Reconsidering. The issue is who or what is going to execute the 2nd resume? What's the
    DX user story there?" The answer was that nobody does and nothing tells the user to. Offered a
    plain line from ghook now and automatic re-registration in the port's hook front door, he
    chose: "let's go with your front-door idea."
    What happens at 7cf05eadb5, traced in the code and not reproduced against a stopped daemon. A
    user resumes a conversation whose row has expired while the daemon is down. The drain archives
    the queued start, because a queued hook never reopens an expired session
    (src/gobby/hooks/replay_fence.py:89-90). The CLI process is new, so ghook holds no session
    credential for it and sends its hooks under the machine key
    (crates/ghook/src/session_binding.rs:52-71). No start pinned it, so each hook is refused with
    403 session_startup_unpinned (src/gobby/hooks/session_claimant.py:397-399). ghook exits 1 with
    a "Daemon error" message on an ordinary hook, 0 for Antigravity, and 2 on a critical hook,
    which for Claude, Codex, Grok and Droid is session start, session end and pre-compact
    (crates/ghook/src/action.rs:314-394; crates/ghook/src/cli_config.rs:32-69). The conversation
    runs without rules, task gates, memory or tracking until that CLI sends another session start.
    The map author's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. HOOK-3, which ports the hook
    claim, builds the recovery: a machine-key hook that is not a start, from a kernel-attested CLI
    that names a conversation with no live row and no pin and whose newest row is expired,
    re-registers that conversation to that CLI where Python refuses it. This is a deliberate
    difference from Python, so HOOK-3 records it as one in its parity evidence. Python and ghook
    are not changed for it before then, and no message is added to ghook. The replay fence keeps
    its policy. HOOK-3's design settles these before it builds:
    - why #25905 made a hook with no pin create nothing, and what the new case leaves of that;
    - the stored owner of the expired row: today only the same process brings a row back, and
      another process replaces an owner only after proving it dead;
    - a resume made inside a CLI that still stores another live row, which today reads as a CLI
      that left the conversation (#26072, #26073);
    - revival refuses a row whose recorded CLI has exited, and the take-over swaps only the pid
      and start time, so ownership and the rest of the terminal context move before the row
      comes back.
27. How the port runs (Josh, 2026-11-16 ~09:20 CT, in session gobby#19401's own pane). Earlier
    he had said "we're not bound by runbooks, and we have the snapshot of the database and the
    code is in github" and "so we can run with minimal friction if it gets the job done. I was
    thinking about letting Fable orchestrate and spawn haiku-5.5-xhigh agents with ultracode".
    Now: "I think I want an Assistant, a default agent running Fable 5.2, and that agent using
    Haiku 5.5 xhigh subagents with ultracode. I need to make sure there isn't any friction from
    runbooks affecting default agents or subagents." After the check below: "I don't need an
    assistant exactly, I just need a default session that isn't Fable that I can get status
    updates from and ask questions", then "over telegram". Then: "could we create a different
    project operating policy for the port based on our discussion?" and, shown its text, "ok
    proceed".
    The map author's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. One default session on
    Fable 5.2 runs the port. It fans work out through the Workflow tool to Haiku 5.5 subagents
    at xhigh effort. A second default session on another model is registered with Telegram. It
    reads tasks, commits and the port session's status line, and passes Josh's questions and
    instructions to the port session with send_message. No runbook, pipeline or spawned seat
    takes part, and no Orchestrator, Triage, Consolidator or Assistant exists. This replaces
    the dispatcher and static seats that docs/port/protocol.md and D116 prescribe; their text
    is not rewritten. The general project operating policy
    (.gobby/workflows/rules/project-operating-policy.yaml, 5.0.0) says so to every default
    session, and its Friction line files nothing under #25603 or #25662 and launches no team.
    What the check found at 7b25460b0d:
    - read and observed: a session whose definition resolves evaluates the mandatory rules
      plus that definition's selectors (src/gobby/workflows/engine/rule_selection.py:110-139);
      the installed default row selects no runbook tag; session gobby#19401, itself default,
      lists none of the 24 runbook-tagged rules as active; the installed tags of two of the 24
      were read;
    - read: a native subagent shares its parent's session, so it meets the parent's rules, and
      the handoff tools are refused to it (rule block-subagent-session-handoff);
    - read: the three scheduled pipelines only wake one live seat of a named type or deliver
      an existing digest file; no seat was live and no session held the Telegram conversation;
    - read, not run: registering a default session with Telegram (the gobby skill's
      references/communications/routing.md), and the wildcard capability passing the
      Assistant's spawner check, which the comment in its definition disagrees with;
    - not observed: several subagents editing and committing in one checkout under one
      session, and the Workflow tool's worktree isolation. The port session tries each on one
      small leaf before fanning out.
28. The port session widens its own fan-out (Josh, 2026-11-18 ~22:35 CT, over Telegram to the port
    session gobby#22918). Asked whether to open several port sessions per wave so that more chunks
    run at once: "Happy to, but I thought you could do that yourself with ultra code."
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. The one port session of
    ruling 27 runs every unblocked chunk of a wave at once through the Workflow tool, each chunk in
    its own worktree. Subagents may make the red and green commits inside their chunk's worktree;
    the port session rules, validates and lands. No further port session is opened unless the port
    session asks for one. D23's cap on concurrent chunk sessions does not limit this fan-out; its
    text is not rewritten.
29. No version bump per landing (Josh, same exchange). Asked to drop the per-landing bump: "Yes. I
    thought we fixed that when I ruled that we only care about the version checks during CI."
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. A landing on 0.9.0 carries
    no managed-binary version bump, lockfile version entry or managed pin. Each changed managed
    binary is bumped once, in the cutover that promotes it, because the promotion guard needs a new
    version only at promotion. A local hook or land_commit check that still demands a bump per
    commit is friction to switch off or fix. This overrides D39's per-commit bump, protocol step
    8's "lands and bumps" and the protocol's "a landing bumps only gdaemon"; their text is not
    rewritten. AGENTS.md's Architecture Facts paragraph is corrected with this ruling.
30. No observation windows until final validation (Josh, same exchange): "We don't need observation
    windows until after we're done with the port. There are no users except me. We need to be
    agile." Also, ~22:20 CT, on node key storage and keypair ideas: "No side quests until after the
    port."
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. A cutover keeps its backup
    (ruling 17), its announcement, its promotion and its rollback recipe. It has no timed
    observation window and no landing freeze for one: work continues once the cutover's smoke
    checks pass. Windows return at the port's final validation, which Josh starts. This overrides
    the window text of rulings 16 and 17 and the map's window hours until he says otherwise; their
    text is not rewritten. Ideas that the port does not need are recorded as found-work lines and
    built after it.
31. Platforms (Josh, 2026-11-18, over Telegram to the port session). ~19:50 CT: "I'd like to be
    compatible with Linux and windows x64 and windows arm". ~19:55 CT: "End product, 1.0 needs to
    work on any os". ~20:00 CT: "Build it like we want compatibility but we can live smoke test
    after the port. Only macOS tests need to pass 100% right now".
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. The 1.0 product targets
    macOS, Linux, Windows x64 and Windows arm64. A chunk's gate is green macOS tests. Product code
    is written for the other platforms: no Unix-only assumption without a cfg branch, and every
    contract a chunk freezes is platform-neutral or carries a recorded line per platform. No chunk
    waits on a Linux container run or a Windows proof; each unproven leg is a record line for the
    smoke test after the port. This overrides the map wherever it says Windows is after 1.0
    (docs/port/map-v2.md:685, :3085, :3163); its text is not rewritten.
32. Python flaws and features (Josh, 2026-11-18, over Telegram to the port session). ~13:00 CT:
    "If it's broken python I don't want to spend a lot of time fixing it. I'd rather note the flaw
    and make sure it doesn't carry over to the rust port." ~16:20 CT: "to clarify, if it's a
    feature that doesn't work that doesn't mean cut the feature, it means don't port the bug".
    ~16:40 CT: "Yeah don't cut features without asking". ~17:25 CT: "The port is yours to handle.
    My admonition was on dropping features in gobby."
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. A flaw in Python that the
    port replaces is fixed only when the fix is cheap or the flaw is a regression the chunk made;
    otherwise it becomes a debt line that names the defect and what Rust must do. A broken feature
    is still ported, working. No Gobby product feature is dropped or narrowed without asking Josh.
    Port tooling, harness and process decisions are the port session's to rule on and report.
33. Worker and node credentials (Josh, 2026-11-18 ~21:38 to ~22:40 CT, over Telegram to the port
    session). He asked whether the API key is used after the worker handshake and what the hub and
    node do; the port session answered, and he replied "Ok just wanted confirmation". Later: "If I
    ever sell a cloud hosted option I will have nodes on machines I don't control."
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. The worker protocol's
    credential covers the handshake only, so R1 keeps the worker channel bound to one connection
    and private to the machine, or adds a per-command credential under a new protocol version.
    That trust model is never reused for a network link. A node sends its API key on every request
    to the hub in 1.0. No chunk freezes a contract that blocks replacing the node credential
    later: the hub derives identity from the credential at one front-door seam, never from the
    request body. Tenant-scoped keys, rotation, a public-CA certificate and keystore storage are
    record lines, built after the port (ruling 30).
34. Push at cutovers (Josh, 2026-11-18 ~14:15 CT, over Telegram to the port session): "we don't
    have to push every few commits. wait until cutovers".
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. A chunk lands on local
    0.9.0 with land_commit and is not pushed. 0.9.0 is pushed to origin at each cutover. This
    replaces the push in protocol step 8; its text is not rewritten.
35. Agent tiers and Codex review (Josh, 2026-11-19, over Telegram to the port session): "No, opus
    medium performs better" and "Are you using the codex code-reviewers?"
    The port session's reading, which Josh corrects where it is wrong. Implementer and fix
    agents run Opus 5.5 at medium effort; per-commit verifiers stay Haiku 5.5 at xhigh. Four
    Codex code-reviewer sessions that Josh opened (sol-6.2, medium effort) review every chunk's
    plan, its checkpoints and its final SHA, and no chunk lands before its Codex verdict on the
    final SHA. Implementers record an exact-SHA test attestation and an evidence file for each
    commit they make. This amends ruling 27's Haiku-only fan-out; its text is not rewritten.

### GEN-ROLLBACK: Generation-retaining promotion, the window hold and gobby rollback

- kind: `infra`
- depends on: `TMUX-PURGE`
- python edits: Promotion modules (bin_set_coherence.py, bin_release_identity.py, bin_freshness_promotion.py), the gclient, gmcp and gterm install paths, cutover's retention preflight and its gmcp and gterm promotion, the --hold-token option on gobby cutover and gobby install, the landing freeze's owner and its clear and re-set refusal (set_landing_freeze in src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/tasks/_landing.py and the admission in src/gobby/tasks/land_commit.py and landing_policy.py), hub-backup's protected-run admission, with its pending-handoff guard and restart intent, and fetch_protected_runs' non-200 rule, and a new src/gobby/cli/rollback.py. These modules change every session's next promotion or landing at once, and they preserve behavior apart from the added retention, with no hold, no freeze owner and no burned version by default; the embedded-input fingerprint is inert until a crate declares one. The hub-backup admission and the non-200 rule are ruling 6 bug fixes unless Josh classes them otherwise at map approval. The rest is Python beyond bug fixes and friction (ruling 6) and a new task (ruling 10), so it needs Josh's word at map approval (D38, D4).

### P0-HARNESS: Corpus v3, replay driver and full-daemon test harness

- kind: `oracle`
- depends on: `GEN-ROLLBACK`, `P0-RECORD`
- python edits: tests/ and scripts/ only.

#### Owned rows

- `HTTP DELETE /api/admin/test/unregister-agent/{run_id}`
- `HTTP POST /api/admin/test/register-agent`
- `HTTP POST /api/admin/test/register-project`
- `HTTP POST /api/admin/test/set-session-usage`

#### Debt not to port

<!-- gport:kept debt:P0-HARNESS -->
- Python starts the isolated e2e daemon in its own session wrongly at tests/e2e/conftest.py (`_start_daemon_instance`, `start_new_session=True`), so when the pytest process is killed the daemon and its worker keep running until a later e2e run's `_cleanup_orphan_gobby_processes` reaps them, and that cleanup reaches only processes that carry the e2e owner markers, after the owner has exited; the Rust port must stop the daemon and its worker when the owning test process dies and on every other exit path, as `GroupWatch` in `crates/gtest-support/src/full_daemon/process.rs` does on Unix.
- Python answers `SERVER_NOT_FOUND` to a call to the native server `gobby` that the proxy does not route, including the native meta-tool `list_tools`, wrongly at src/gobby/servers/routes/mcp/tools.py (line 58, under the `/api/mcp` prefix at line 52, which sends `POST /api/mcp/{server_name}/tools/{tool_name}` to `mcp_proxy`; the refusal is at src/gobby/mcp_proxy/services/tool_execution.py lines 342-352); the Rust port must not return `SERVER_NOT_FOUND` for a tool the native server serves; GW-2 decides the REST route's answer for the native server (docs/port/map-v2.md, GW-2).
<!-- gport:end debt:P0-HARNESS -->

### P0-DECISIONS: Hook-decision corpus, known-bad registry and regex audit

- kind: `oracle`
- depends on: `P0-HARNESS`
- python edits: tests/ only (recorder, corpus, registry JSON).

#### Owned rows

#### Debt not to port

<!-- gport:kept debt:P0-DECISIONS -->
- Python's rule-pass `run_command` never runs, wrongly, at src/gobby/workflows/engine/run_command.py (`build_run_command_payload`, line 137): it deep-copies the frozen event data, `_ReadOnlyDict.__deepcopy__` (src/gobby/workflows/evaluation_context.py, line 19) returns the same read-only object, `normalize_tool_fields` then pops from it and raises `TypeError("Workflow evaluation inputs are read-only")`, and the effect fails open at src/gobby/workflows/engine/run_command_effects.py lines 76-78; the Rust port must build the payload from a mutable copy and run the command (known-bad row `known-bad-d45a6fbd1961`).
- Python's MCP proxy builds a second before_tool event with no agent id for a call that a hook already admitted, wrongly at src/gobby/mcp_proxy/services/result_handling.py (`build_before_tool_event`, line 32; it only sets `_mcp_proxy_duplicate_before_tool` at line 163), and the pending tool context it could copy from holds no agent id (src/gobby/workflows/tool_context.py, `_snapshot_tool_context`, line 107), so a rule on a missing agent id treats a subagent's admitted call as the parent's; the Rust port's pending context keeps the agent identity and the duplicate event inherits it, or the call is not evaluated a second time (known-bad row `known-bad-d4e670a9a8cf`).
- Python's rewrite recheck calls `_check_agent_tool_enforcement` without `base_merge_write` at src/gobby/workflows/engine/core.py lines 747-752, while the first check passes it (lines 552-558). Read from the code, not reproduced by a case; the Rust port does not copy the omission without a decision.
- The shell-guard rows of the known-bad index (#24177, #24298 x5, #24299 x2, #24772 and the uv lock and uv sync row) are Python flaws: the Rust port implements each row's `correct` value, never `python_observed` (D18).
- Python `re` accepts lookaround; the Rust `regex` crate rejects it. The audit report (docs/port/regex-audit.json) names every pattern with its rewrite or its reported decision; the Rust port copies no lookaround syntax and takes the rewrite or the operator's ruling for each reported row.
- Python `re` reads `\d`, `\w`, `\b` and `\B` from the interpreter's Unicode tables, which change between minors (`\d` 680 code points on CPython 3.13 and 760 on 3.14, `\w` 138,558 and 142,940; docs/port/regex-audit.json `decisions`), and `\B` on the empty subject matches on 3.14 and not on 3.13 (tests/contracts/regex_audit/constructs.json, `boundary`). The Rust port assumes no parity on these atoms until the operator rules on each `-version-difference` decision.
- Python's substitution accepts an empty match directly after a non-empty one (`x*` replaced by `-` in `abxd` gives `-a-b--d-`, case `consumer-sub-empty-match` in tests/contracts/regex_audit/consumers.json), and Python replacement templates expand group references (`consumer-sub-template`); the port's regex_replace must reproduce both, not the Rust iterator's or template syntax's own answer.
- `HookResponse.trigger_action` has no producer in Python (src/gobby/hooks/events.py line 287, declared for future use), and `modify_args` has no reachable producer: only the ACP base adapter writes it (src/gobby/adapters/acp_hook_adapter.py lines 363-368), for BeforeModel and BeforeToolSelection, which no routed adapter reaches. The Rust port adds no producer of either without a contract change.
- `EventHandlers` takes a `call_tool` collaborator (src/gobby/hooks/event_handlers/__init__.py line 133) that no handler reads; the Rust port needs no such collaborator.
- Python `re`'s `$` without MULTILINE also matches before a final newline; the Rust `regex` crate's `$` matches only at the end. The audit rewrites a final `$` on a BOOL row to `(?:\n?\Z)` (family D1, 9 rows), reports any other such `$` as `dollar-before-final-newline` and a lookaround body that can match `\n` as `lookaround-newline-body` (tests/contracts/regex_audit_classify.py lines 435-509; no report row has either reason). The Rust port takes the report's text for each `$`, never Rust's `$` in place of Python's (chunk plan D-14).
- Python `re`'s `\s` matches U+001C to U+001F; the Rust `regex` crate's Unicode `\s` does not. The audit's T-DOM rewrites spell Python's set as an explicit class (`[\x{9}-\x{D}\x{1C}-\x{20}...]` in docs/port/regex-audit.json; tests/contracts/test_regex_audit.py lines 1874-1878), so the Rust port never uses its bare `\s` for a Python `\s`.
- The assistant-response consumer searches each allowlisted pattern under `re.IGNORECASE` and returns the matched slice, not a boolean (src/gobby/workflows/assistant_response_matching.py lines 110 and 115; consumer class SPAN-ALLOWLIST). Case folding is part of that span, so the Rust port keeps Python's folding and the returned span, not only whether it matched.
- A rewrite that consumes characters the lookaround or `$` did not (families R1, R2+ and D1) changes spans, captures and fullmatch results, so the audit gives one only to a BOOL row: all 16 such rows (32 family tags) are BOOL, and a lookaround on any other consumer is reported `lookaround-non-boolean` (`test_consuming_rewrites_go_only_to_boolean_consumers`, tests/contracts/test_regex_audit.py line 992; chunk plan D-11). The Rust port of a non-BOOL row keeps the original's span, captures and fullmatch result.
<!-- gport:end debt:P0-DECISIONS -->

### A1-CONFIG: A1-CONFIG: config model, reads and writes (#21559 P5a)

- kind: `family`
- depends on: `A1-RUNTIME`, `R2`, `REC-PILOT`
- python edits: The port adds no Python of its own. This chunk lands each plan Python edit that fixes a defect in today's behavior on 0.9.0 under D4's bug-fix clause (rulings 6 and 15), each as its own commit with its test, re-recording the cases it changes (D18): ChatConfig.validate_default_mode renders its choices sorted, because it renders a set whose order changes with each process's string hashing (src/gobby/config/features.py:356-363); validate_feature_candidates re-raises only when an error's msg names the removed local: selector, not on str(exc), which includes pydantic's truncated input display (src/gobby/config/feature_base.py:187-203); validate_nightly_repair_timezone accepts exactly the tzdata package's zone names, with tzdata listed in pyproject.toml for every platform and uv.lock updated, because ZoneInfo accepts a name by the host's zone files, so a name valid on one host fails on another (src/gobby/config/code_index.py:184-193); a stored name outside the list would then fail validation, so that commit lands only once the hub's stored code_index.nightly_repair_timezone is unset or in the list; and patch_flat authorizes unset keys in sorted order (src/gobby/config/values.py:357-477; gdaemon-runtime-family plan 5.8 and 5.9). An edit that only aligns Python with the native contract is not made, and the native difference is triaged under D17 as an accepted contract change: the self-parsed request bodies of 5.9 and 5.12 (native answers a malformed body with the plan's fixed 422 detail, Python with FastAPI's validation list), the stable location-and-message validation messages at sites 1 and 4 (the plan's Validation messages decision), and 5.11's YAML loader and dumper (the D18 rows in the goal). 5.8's deletion of validate_injection_format is not made either: the field's Literal type rejects every value first (src/gobby/config/skills.py:102, :146-153), so the deletion fixes nothing, and the native side ports only the Literal's message. 5.7's backup_path retyping is not made, so the native per-key validator stores memory_backup.backup_path as Python's Path validation does (src/gobby/config/persistence.py:683).

An edit that only aligns Python with the native contract is not made, and the native difference is an accepted contract change in D17's triage: the self-parsed request bodies of 5.4, 5.10, 5.14 and 5.16, 5.4's frontmatter refusal and JSON members, and the stable location-and-message validation messages at sites 2 and 3. 5.10.6's project_id pass-through in Python's SaveSecretRequest and set_named_secret is not made either (D4): native accepts no project_id until MUX-1 lands the field's native half, so it is no contract difference at this checkpoint.

## Frozen seam contract

<!-- gport:kept seam-contract -->
Contract of the SEAM-FAMILY chunk (#25950), as built on its branch. The frozen types, traits and signatures are: the claim shape (`Credential::AfterAuthenticate`, `Gate`, `PeerRule::LoopbackOnly` as the canonical loopback test, and equal path specificity going to the lower `start_rank`, then registry order); `FrontDoorState.context` and `run_claims(&mut FrontDoorState, ...)`; the `RouteFamily` hooks `before_spawn`, `after_healthy`, `recovery_complete` and `start_rank`, with `SpawnKind`, and `deactivate` safe on a partly started or activated family; the family context (`ContextParts` with `publisher`, `startup`, `extensions`; `FamilyContext` with `config_publisher`, `startup`, `extension`, `unconfigured`), `Extensions`, `StartupRecorder`, `ConfigEpochPublisher` (a struct with a private sender, behind an `Arc`, whose `reload` reads and publishes under one lock), `DegradedServices` and `FamilyLogger` (both with private fields, and the logger counters); `JobSink` with the required `validate_job` and `remove_job` and the defaulted `statuses`; `start_families(entries, ctx, unwind_budget)` and `activate_families(entries, ctx, jobs, unwind_budget)`, whose fatal unwind deactivates the failing family first and counts its one deadline from the moment the unwind begins, and `deactivate_families(entries, mode, deadline)`; the telemetry owned labels, `set_gauge` and `SampleValue::Gauge`; `FAMILIES` (multi-line, one family per line) and `activation_plan(families, routes)`; `PrepareInputs` (families, extensions, startup), `prepare` with three sequential reads and `start_families`, `ProcessFamilies` with `config_read` and `start_warnings`, and `Disposition` with `disposition(&StartFailure)`; `spawn_config_poller(source, Arc<ConfigEpochPublisher>, every, first_read)` and `activate_after_first_read`, so routed logging activates only after a successful configuration read; `logging::activate(settings, backend_command)` taking its rotation owner from `writer::owner_for`; `settings::from_values(values, previous, defaults)`, where an absent key takes its activation default and an invalid one keeps the previous setting; `logging::classify::surface_for_target` returning `Option<Surface>`; the logging test seams; `SizeRotatingFile::open` opening in append mode, with rollover counting a line in characters as CPython's `RotatingFileHandler` does.

### Frozen files

- `crates/gcore/src/database_concurrency.rs`
- `crates/gcore/src/database_concurrency/pool_budget.rs`
- `crates/gcore/src/domain_error.rs`
- `crates/gcore/src/family/config_epoch.rs`

## Known-bad index

<!-- gport:kept known-bad -->
- Registry: `tests/contracts/rules_known_bad.json` (P0-DECISIONS), 13 rows, each with a control input that Python gets right. Python's replay asserts `python_observed`; a port asserts `correct` (D18). Each row's `ref`, entry, input, field, Python's value and the correct value:
  - `known-bad-b36c9397a142` #24177, `gobby.hooks.normalization.normalize_tool_fields`, `bash -lc "echo x | { cd sub; }; touch z.py"`, `canonical_repo_mutation_scope_unknown`: null, correct true.
  - `known-bad-40bc71234c2c`, `known-bad-13ed5b7663cb`, `known-bad-e65a7b801e9c`, `known-bad-aa3c452615e6`, `known-bad-4364114463a3` #24298, `gobby.workflows.git_commit_parsing.parse_git_commit_invocations`, field `count`: 0, correct 1, for `Git commit -m x foreign.txt`, `/usr/bin/GIT commit -m x foreign.txt`, `env -S 'git commit -m x foreign.txt'`, `trap 'git commit -m x foreign.txt' EXIT` and `echo commit -m x foreign.txt | xargs git`.
  - `known-bad-1f79ed390d5a`, `known-bad-b95a55516a5c` #24299, `gobby.hooks.provider_launch_guard.blocks_direct_provider_launch`, field `result`: false, correct true, for `repeat 1 claude -p hi` and `for i j (1 2) claude -p hi`.
  - `known-bad-72f46275132c` #24772, `gobby.workflows.approval_brace_guard.approval_command_word_expansion`, a git alias that runs `gobby se$()ssions` through `GIT_CONFIG_*`, field `result`: false, correct true.
  - `known-bad-4ceb0723b8f9`, `known-bad-857c833813e4` unfiled:uv-lock-sync-scope-unknown, `gobby.hooks.normalization.normalize_tool_fields`, `uv lock` and `uv sync`, `canonical_repo_mutation_scope_unknown`: true, correct false.
  - `known-bad-d45a6fbd1961` unfiled:frozen-run-command-payload, `gobby.workflows.engine.run_command.build_run_command_payload`, `echo hi` on a frozen event, field `raised`: `TypeError`, correct null. ### GEN-ROLLBACK

<!-- gport:kept found:GEN-ROLLBACK -->
- Python install flaws left in place by #26108 (Josh, 2026-11-18: note the flaw, do not carry it into Rust). The Rust install and rollback path must not repeat them:
  - A probe of a candidate or a generation copy runs `--version`, and a ghook older than 0.9.88 rewrites the live `.ghook-runtime.json` when it does (`bin_restore.py` `_member_refusal`, `check_binary_release` preflight). A read-only check must write nothing; the installer alone writes the stamp, after the swap, under the store lock.
  - `ghook --version` from the installed path writes the stamp without the store lock, so a probe that started before a swap can overwrite the new stamp. The stamp file's bytes also differ by a trailing newline depending on the writer. One writer, one encoding.
  - Cutover captures the candidate's runtime stamp after the daemon stops, so an unusable candidate is refused with the daemon down. Every candidate check belongs before the stop.
  - The outgoing receipt is removed before the binary replace and put back on failure; a crash between the two loses it. Replace both atomically or write the new receipt first.
  - A restore failure message names replaced and unreplaced members and omits the burned versions.
  - The async hook dispatcher (`hooks/mcp_dispatch.py`) and the sync one (`hooks/dispatchers/mcp.py`) still differ: rewriting a resolved ref into tool arguments, an explicit unresolvable ref, a full UUID with no store, the `current` alias, project scoping, and `_proxy` routing. Rust gets one dispatcher.
  - A gmcp settle failure skips writing the daemon start command, and a rerun that finds gmcp current does not write it either.
  - `tests/fixtures/landing_case.py` names bundled seats in helper names (AGENTS rule 16).
- Tests that fail on 0.9.0 and predate #26108, not fixed: `tests/agents/test_srt_socket_root_variable.py::test_spawn_records_the_launch_socket_root_on_the_child_session`, `tests/workflows/test_base_merge_rules.py::test_team_worktree_scope_is_static_in_srt_launch_policy[no-capability]`, and an order-dependent failure in `tests/agents/test_team_teardown_epic_leaf.py`. All three cover spawned-seat launch, which ruling 27 retires for the port.
<!-- gport:end found:GEN-ROLLBACK -->

### P0-RECORD

<!-- gport:kept found:P0-RECORD -->
- `docs/contracts/parity-ledger-status.d/daemon-config-auth-infra.json`, `docs/contracts/parity-ledger.md`, `docs/port/ledger.md`: GEN-ROLLBACK added twelve `gobby rollback` verbs with no status entry, so the Python generator failed and the ledger could not be reproduced. Classified and regenerated in 1844e047ee; the frozen ledger went from 1,094 to 1,106 rows, and every row has one owner.
- The map text still says 1,094 rows in several places (this chunk's goal, D5 and others). The count in force is the frozen ledger's, 1,106. The map is not edited here; a map revision takes the correction.
- gport refuses selectors it cannot prove match as Python `re` does: backreferences, named groups, verbose mode, comment groups, `\B`, and case-insensitive matching with non-ASCII characters are bad selectors, as are a few Python-valid forms (possessive quantifiers, atomic groups, look-behinds with alternation, nested sets). A selector that uses the ignore-case flag, `\w`, `\W` or `\b` fails the run with exit 2 when it meets a non-ASCII key. No selector in the map uses any of these and every ledger key is ASCII. A chunk that needs one rewrites the selector.
- `gport shapes` refuses a daemon endpoint that is not loopback before it attaches the operator token. `gport shapes` and `gport start` refuse a redirect from the daemon with exit 2 and percent-encode ids in request paths. A `tool_name` or `server_name` value that is shaped like an identifier prints as the row label, so a secret passed in that field would show; no key or value text prints otherwise.
- Census limits. The cache section lists every assigned name ending in `_cache`, so about ten rows hold a path or a loaded value and no cache. It misses targets reached through a call or a subscript (`make().x_cache = ...`), identifiers with non-ASCII letters, and dotted names split by spaces or a line continuation; the tree has none. The invalidation column does not count a call inside an f-string field, and it counts `NOTIFY` in a comment.
<!-- gport:end found:P0-RECORD -->

The supervisor_write_token row names its getter, but the code that stores the token is _TokenLine.feed (src/gobby/runner_backend.py:110-122).
- Platform: the Rust lifecycle backend module (crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/mod.rs:10-11), the singleton-lock module pid_file (mod.rs:3-4) and the BackendSupervisor handshake methods (crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_handshake.rs:565-566) are cfg(unix). The Windows supervisor, the Windows singleton lock and the Windows handshake transport are unbuilt, and the map names no chunk for them.
- Platform: the operator's 2026-11-18 19:50 CT ruling makes Windows x64 and arm64 1.0 targets, and the 19:55 CT ruling requires 1.0 to work on any OS. docs/port/map-v2.md:685 (under R-RETIRE, risks), :3085 and :3163 (table rows) still say Windows is post-1.0. The map must change, and the owner of the Windows work is open for the port session.
- Platform: the census guard reads the operator's home from the passwd entry on Linux and macOS (pwd.getpwuid, tests/e2e/task_census_bootstrap.py:126-134) and from Path.home() on win32; the Linux and Windows branches are unproven, and the post-port smoke test runs the guard on each.
- Platform: the supervisor pipe reader has a Windows branch (msvcrt.open_osfhandle, src/gobby/runner_backend.py:138-141) that no run has exercised; the Windows x64 and arm64 legs are unproven.
- Platform: the inherited singleton-lock descriptor (GOBBY_SINGLETON_LOCK_FD, src/gobby/runner_pid_file.py:42) is read as an integer by adopt_inherited_claim (:509-519) with no Windows branch; src/gobby/runner.py:620-621 clears the variable only off Windows. The R0 review did not run this on Windows; the post-port smoke test covers it.
- Platform: the cross-target compile checks for x86_64-pc-windows-msvc and aarch64-pc-windows-msvc (crates/gdaemon/Cargo.toml) prove nothing from the macOS port host: `cargo check -p gobby-daemon --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` stops in the build scripts of openssl-sys and ring, which need a Windows C toolchain, before any gdaemon code compiles; the arm64 target was not tried. The Windows compile proof waits for the post-port smoke test on a Windows host (ruling 31).
- Integers on the worker wire stay within [-2^63, 2^64-1]; src/gobby/worker/encode.py refuses others (WIRE_INT_MIN and WIRE_INT_MAX at :26-27, encode_value at :34-62), and Int in src/gobby/worker/decode.py refuses them (:40-45).
- A create that returns before every phase ran is answered failed with error type IncompleteCreate, and the gate becomes unusable (src/gobby/worker/phases.py:142-151).
- A schema identity that cannot be loaded answers op_failed ("schema identity unavailable") and binds no generation, because the handshake builds its result before it binds the generation (src/gobby/worker/facade.py:100-112).
- The Rust client checks the handshake result and every initialize reply against the contract: the five initialize keys, the status values, the exact successor phase on ready, a non-empty error_type on failed, and the fixed text of each of the ten error codes (crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_handshake.rs).
- The census e2e module skips on the Ubuntu CI legs (.github/workflows/ci.yml:247 runs-on ubuntu-latest, matrix Python 3.13 and 3.14 at :261; census_skip_reason, tests/e2e/test_runner_task_census.py:31-33), and the macOS leg at .github/workflows/ci.yml:416-422 does not run the census, so CI never checks the lifecycle census.
- The census e2e module skips as a whole off the recording host, including its guard tests (pytestmark, tests/e2e/test_runner_task_census.py:31-33). The same guard logic runs on every host in tests/contracts/test_runner_census_recorder.py.
- The census match is load-sensitive: under host load the loop-default submitter list gains five names (four InteractiveAttentionMonitor helpers and HostUpgradeCoordinator._installed_sha256). TIMING_OPTIONAL_SUBMITTERS (tests/e2e/task_census_recorder.py:54) and timing_comparison_view (:571-583) compare those five as optional and every other row exactly; the rule and its cause are in docs/contracts/runner-lifecycle.md:181-205. Before the rule, 2 of 2 loaded runs failed and 0 of 9 quiet runs failed; the verifier also saw one failure in 3 combined runs.
- The Rust runner's census comparison of the loop's default executor needs the same five-name rule (tests/e2e/task_census_recorder.py:54).
- An unexplained sandbox_reaper difference was reported once and did not reproduce in 11 boots (2 under load, 9 quiet), so sweep_sandbox_run_roots (tests/contracts/runner_census.json:252) stays compared exactly. The boot counts come from the R0 verification runs and are not repo-checkable.
- The census bootstrap guard reads bootstrap.yaml as the daemon does (yaml.safe_load, last duplicate key wins; tests/e2e/task_census_bootstrap.py:87), so a file the daemon refuses is refused by the guard.
- The census guard compares the test-hub DSN by parsed parts, with host 127.0.0.1 or localhost accepted (tests/e2e/task_census_bootstrap.py:49, database_url_is_test_hub).
- A create that enters a phase out of order is cancelled and awaited before it is answered failed with PhaseOrderViolation (src/gobby/worker/phases.py:159 and :163-176, _settle_out_of_order). The gate is unusable afterwards, and initialize never waits forever.
- Four task sites with no stop owner have no row in tests/contracts/runner_census.json or tests/contracts/runner_lifecycle.json: the memory projection repair and the embedding lease rebuild (src/gobby/runner_init/services.py:401-402 and :443-445), and the agent broadcast and terminal lifecycle tasks (src/gobby/runner_broadcasting.py:65-68 and :109-112). The census row memory-projection-scope-repair comes from MemoryManager.schedule_background_task (src/gobby/memory/manager.py:310), a different path. The inventory rows at tests/contracts/runner_lifecycle.json:1876 and :2280 belong to other tasks (the message processor and MCP keepalive). The census fixture is frozen, so the port session chooses the inventory or a new fixture for these rows.
- The synchronous GobbyRunner.__init__ (src/gobby/runner.py:261-270) has no caller in src/gobby. Tests call it (tests/test_runner_init.py, tests/test_runner_lifecycle.py, tests/test_runner_shutdown.py), and its path reaches the memory services (tests/contracts/runner_lifecycle.json:127). It is not a defect; whether the port keeps it is open for the port session.
- src/gobby/runner.py:171 declares _pending_tasks, and src/gobby/runner_init/storage.py:191 sets it to an empty set. No code in src/gobby reads it; tests/test_runner_init.py:325 asserts it is empty. It is not a defect; whether the port keeps the attribute is open for the port session.
- The static phase pin cannot attribute three construct seams: main (src/gobby/runner.py:610, the process entry point that no construct phase names; tests/contracts/runner_lifecycle.json:118), MemoryManager.schedule_background_task (src/gobby/memory/manager.py:310, reached through a dict value narrowed by isinstance; :127), and DefinitionRevisionListener.start (reached through getattr at src/gobby/runner_init/services.py:75; :140). These are limits of the pin, not Python defects. The Rust runner must reach each seam by a direct call that its phase pin follows, and main needs its own pin wording.

### R1

<!-- gport:kept found:R1 -->
- From R0: after the handshake, initialize and invoke carry no credential, and generations are sequential and not secret (docs/contracts/worker-protocol.md, Credential and handshake, line 74). R1 must keep the worker channel connection-bound and private to the machine, or add a per-command credential under a new protocol version (operator, 2026-11-18 21:38 CT). Closed in R1 at bd210a7e591b9c3843b77306d3cce43fc0ce374c (the Rust transport): each spawn carries its own credential on its own inherited pipe, and the connection is a socket pair whose parent end is close-on-exec, so it is bound to one child and reachable through no listener, path or port. No per-command credential and no new protocol version were added.
- From R0: docs/contracts/worker-protocol.md has no per-command error-code table. The invoke row of the guard order (Commands and guard order, line 112) lists invalid_request twice and omits op_failed, and the Unknown outcome section (:243-253) does not say that an error code outside a command's set fails validation. R1 closes these. Closed in R1 at c4a879ef566be0c51e16ed234d86c92214885204: the contract has the per-command error-code table and the out-of-set rule, and `tests/contracts/test_worker_protocol_doc.py` pins the table to the code sets.
- From R0: src/gobby/storage/schema_contract.py:33-36 reads the packaged schema identity on every call, and src/gobby/worker/facade.py:220 calls it while the handshake result is built, so every handshake reads the file. R1 may cache it. Closed in R1 at f9827306815ac65b5a83d63852465202ccd84e34: the facade reads the packaged identity through `schema_identity()` in `src/gobby/worker/identity.py`, a process-wide cache that never caches a failed read.
- From R0, for R1 and later: the inventory pins in tests/contracts/test_runner_lifecycle_inventory.py re-read src/gobby on every run. A chunk whose edit moves a seam to another phase updates tests/contracts/runner_lifecycle.json in the same commit; tests/contracts/runner_census.json is frozen, and a mismatch means stop and report. Closed for R1 as a no-op: R1 moves no seam, so `tests/contracts/runner_lifecycle.json` and `tests/contracts/test_runner_lifecycle_inventory.py` are unchanged. The rule stays in force for later chunks.
- From R0: opaque invoke results are not checked for integer width. worker_handshake.rs:268 holds an invoke result as serde_json::Value, and crates/gdaemon/Cargo.toml:46 enables no arbitrary_precision feature, so serde_json turns an integer literal beyond 64 bits into an f64. Python refuses such integers on the wire, but the Rust client cannot detect them in an opaque result. R1 wires the client and owns the check. Closed in R1 at c4a879ef566be0c51e16ed234d86c92214885204: the reply reader scans the raw reply, and an integer literal outside [-2^63, 2^64 - 1] is rejected before parsing, so it is an unknown outcome for initialize and invoke. No manifest feature changed.
- Decisions:
  - The incompatible-override rejection is the Rust-mode handshake (`handle_handshake_failure` in `crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/backend/handshake.rs`). This departs from the brief's anchor. The criterion that holds today is the malformed or empty-argv refusal in `from_command_json` (`crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/backend.rs`), unchanged; `src/gobby/cli/daemon_start.py` only sets the override and is unchanged. The Python default rejects no handshake until CP-RUNNER-CUT activates Rust mode.
  - Handshake failures are classified by `handshake_outcome`. An unknown outcome is a crash: a backend that gives no answer by its deadline, a closed channel, a framing error or a reply over its limit respawns with backoff and a new generation, with no sticky refusal after consecutive crashes (port session, 2026-11-19). Every other failure (protocol, refused, op_failed, credential missing, request too large, a schema identity or commands mismatch) is sticky and refuses the supervisor with a fixed class reason, never the worker's text.
  - A handshake child that exits with status 78 is refused through the existing exit-78 refusal, which is sticky, and that check runs before the lost-reply crash path. The plan named no exit code for this path; the marker refusal in the Python worker boot exits 78, and that exit must stay sticky under supervision.
  - The worker marker is `GOBBY_WORKER_MARKER` (value "1"), with `GOBBY_WORKER_CHANNEL_FD` and `GOBBY_WORKER_CREDENTIAL_FD`. Only gdaemon sets them, only in Rust mode, and the marker is set in the same spawn step that creates the descriptors, so a child never sees a marker without its descriptors. Python's main pops all three. No production key, flag or argv changed.
  - The linked-worktree daemon guard runs for every start, worker starts included (unchanged). Tests that start a worker from a linked worktree set `GOBBY_ALLOW_WORKTREE_DAEMON=1` on the child only.
  - Framing is a 4-byte big-endian length prefix. Request and reply bounds are 65,536 bytes for handshake and initialize and 16 MiB for invoke. Reply deadlines are 60 s for handshake and 300 s for initialize and invoke. A reply over its bound, or no answer by the deadline, is a channel error: an unknown outcome for initialize and invoke and a crash for handshake. The limits and deadlines are declared in `crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_handshake.rs` beside the request bounds. R2 must bound invoke results at 16 MiB or raise the bound.
  - An unknown outcome on handshake or initialize ends the worker child; the next spawn takes a new generation, and the ended generation is refused as stale. R1 registers no lifecycle phase, so R2 inherits this rule for the phases it registers. The contract's Unknown outcome section states it.
  - The Rust client compares the handshake's schema_identity with `SchemaIdentityContract::embedded()` (gcore) after the commands check; a mismatch is `ResultMismatch(HandshakeField::SchemaIdentity)` and binds no session. `packaged_identity_equals_embedded_contract` (`crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_handshake/tests.rs`) tests the packaged identity and the embedded contract equal.
  - Each spawn draws its generation exactly once: `BackendChild::spawn` draws it and attach takes the drawn `WorkerSession`, not the generation source. `issued_generations()` reports the real count, and `one_spawn_draws_one_generation` pins one draw per spawn. This resolves the double-draw hazard and the stubbed count left by the plan's slice-2 and red slice-3 commits.
  - One orchestration owner per Python start, chosen by the marker alone. A marked start takes the boot in main before the descriptors are sealed; run_gobby refuses a node or an unsupported platform first, then serves the worker without binding the daemon bootstrap, claiming the PID file or constructing GobbyRunner. The proof is process level, in a subprocess (`tests/worker/test_orchestration_owner.py`). The marker-absent case is labelled "pre CP-RUNNER-CUT"; R-RETIRE turns it into a refusal.
  - The import-scope pin in `tests/worker/test_worker_phases.py` (`test_only_runner_imports_worker`) gained the type-only import of `gobby.worker.channel` for run_gobby's worker parameter, and the call-time imports of `gobby.worker.channel` in main and run_gobby. The pin is an exact (module, scope) set that counts function-body imports, and the import stays out of module scope because the dormancy probe requires that importing runner load no worker module.
  - Agent tiers and review (ruling 35): implementer and fix agents run Opus 5.5 at medium effort, per-commit verifiers stay Haiku 5.5 at xhigh, and the operator's four Codex code-reviewer sessions review every chunk's plan, checkpoints and final SHA. No chunk lands before its Codex verdict on the final SHA, and implementers record exact-SHA test attestations with an evidence file for each.
- Contracts:
  - The worker credential is per spawn, rides its own inherited pipe, and is never in argv, the environment, a log or a repr (`WorkerBoot` and `WorkerAttachment` both omit it).
  - `take_worker_boot` (`src/gobby/worker/channel.py`) reads the credential, then checks with fstat that the channel descriptor is open and is a socket. A closed or non-socket channel refuses the marked start with exit 78 and a message naming `GOBBY_WORKER_CHANNEL_FD`, before `socket()` is reached (3f7f95a93fda63f6612a194644671a4c5d5f99db). Both descriptor variables go through one parser that accepts only 3 through 2**31 - 1 (the C int range) and refuses any other value with exit 78 and a message naming the variable (a12c94f6367386c893762789a34cdb6819a93361). It also refuses a marked start on nt with exit 78 before any descriptor is read.
  - docs/contracts/worker-protocol.md has a "Transport and framing" section (45326e421bc938ec0755539e848777d823299077): the socket pair, the three descriptor variables, the per-spawn credential pipe, what `take_worker_boot` refuses (exit 78), the length-prefixed frames with their request and reply limits, and the Rust client's 60 s and 300 s reply deadlines. Its R1 hand-off section points at the section that holds each item, and it states the schema_identity comparison.
  - main passes `worker` to run_gobby on every start, so the exact-call assertion in `tests/test_runner_lifecycle.py` (`test_main_override_env_lets_a_worktree_package_start`) names `worker=None`, and the record_start fake in `tests/test_runner_pid_file.py` accepts `worker`. No other run_gobby fake or assertion changed.
  - The real-Python handshake test asserts that the worker exits and is reaped once its channel closes. It sets `PYTHONPATH`, `DATABASE_URL`, `GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT` and `GOBBY_ALLOW_WORKTREE_DAEMON` on the child only and writes a `bootstrap.yaml` with database_url and files_home, the shape `tests/worker/test_orchestration_owner.py` uses; it sets no `GOBBY_CONFIG`.
  - `python_default_spawn_is_unchanged` (`crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/backend/tests.rs`) pins the full environment name set of a Python-default spawn (the inherited set plus `GOBBY_HOME` and `GOBBY_SUPERVISOR_FD`, less `GOBBY_SINGLETON_LOCK_FD` and `GOBBY_PARENT_FD`) and its argv against the code before this chunk, plus the inherited descriptor set. The shell's own exports and macOS `DYLD_` names are left out on both sides of that comparison.
  - `worker_link_child_end_is_the_only_inherited_channel` lives in `crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/backend/tests.rs`, because `BackendChild::spawn` is private to the backend module; its parent-end check is in `crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_channel/tests.rs`, which calls attach directly. `parent_end_is_close_on_exec` is a tokio test, because `UnixStream::from_std` needs the runtime attach runs in. The credential test compares without printing the credential.
  - The respawn test shortens its own supervisor's drain_timeout to one second: an exited but unreaped child keeps the drain's signal path open for the whole production grace budget (17 seconds), which that test does not exercise.
  - The red slice-3 Rust commit (38ed87afbe) failed `cargo clippy -D warnings` only on dead code in its stubs (`WorkerSpawn`, attach, `read_frame`, `BackendChild::worker`, then always None); bd210a7e59 wired each one, and every green commit passes clippy with no allow attribute.
  - The worker attachment on `BackendChild` is transitional: the red slice-3 Rust commit (38ed87afbe) added `worker: Option<WorkerAttachment>`, always None there, so the credential and real-worker tests compiled and failed on their assertions; bd210a7e59 stores the attachment that attach returns in it.
- Found and fixed in this chunk:
  - `tests/contracts/test_corpus_v3.py` (`test_ledger_and_waiver_manifest_match_the_digests_taken_at_head`) carried a bundled definition name in its assertion message, against AGENTS rule 16 (from #25938). c0895940cc rewords the message; the check is unchanged.
  - Found by the Codex review (gobby#22924) at 3f7f95a93f: the inherited-descriptor parser in `src/gobby/worker/channel.py` accepted any integer of 3 or more, so a value beyond the C int range escaped the StartRefusal path (on Python 3.14 as OverflowError from `os.fstat` for `GOBBY_WORKER_CHANNEL_FD` and as TypeError from `os.fdopen` for `GOBBY_WORKER_CREDENTIAL_FD`). 125fb19b5f5351d2f9dbf233861dd0c4ee4f5b64 adds the failing test `test_take_worker_boot_refuses_a_descriptor_beyond_the_c_int_range`, parametrized over both variables. Closed at a12c94f6367386c893762789a34cdb6819a93361: one shared upper bound, 2**31 - 1, refuses with the existing StartRefusal (exit 78); no except clause was widened.
  - `crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_handshake/reply_tests.rs` carried the design-ruling ids R56 and R66 in two comments. Closed at a12c94f6367386c893762789a34cdb6819a93361: the comments describe the property in words.
  - `tests/worker/test_worker_ops.py` (added by #25948) had 7 test-types findings: attr-defined on decoded object values at lines 127, 165 and 205 to 208, and func-returns-value at line 320. Closed at a12c94f6367386c893762789a34cdb6819a93361: the test narrows the decoded values with `isinstance(..., Window)` and `isinstance(..., WriteRequest)` and calls `require_machine_owned` as a statement, with no type: ignore, cast or baseline edit; `gobby test-types audit tests/worker` exits 0.
- Unproven platform legs (post-port smoke test, ruling 31):
  - The worker transport is unix-only. The non-unix branch of `crates/gdaemon/src/lifecycle/worker_channel.rs` returns `io::ErrorKind::Unsupported` from attach, and `WorkerLink`'s exchange returns a channel error. The worker channel is transitional until CP-PREDELETE-CUT, so no product feature is narrowed. The Windows worker transport is unbuilt.
  - Windows x64 (`x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`): `worker_channel.rs` compiled in an isolated check with its parent types stubbed. With `WorkerSpawn` and attach crate-private again (3f7f95a93f), that check gives 2 dead_code warnings (`WorkerSpawn` never constructed, the non-unix attach never used), because `lifecycle::backend` is cfg(unix). The full gobby-daemon check for that target stops in the ring and openssl-sys build scripts on the macOS port host (no Windows SDK headers, no perl), before the crate compiles. Owner of the warnings: the seam chunk (#25950), which adds the non-unix dead_code allows and the win32 skips in its commit that edits the lifecycle sites. Owner of the run: post-port smoke test.
  - Windows arm64 (`aarch64-pc-windows-msvc`): not checked. No worker test has run on Windows. Owner: post-port smoke test.
  - Linux: the worker tests (`tests/worker`, the Rust lifecycle tests) ran on macOS only. Owner: post-port smoke test.

### NODEHUB

<!-- gport:kept found:NODEHUB -->
- From R0, for NODEHUB and S4-NODE: the worker protocol's trust model is never reused for a network link (operator, 2026-11-18 21:38 CT).
- From R0, for NODEHUB and S4-NODE: node-to-hub calls carry the node API key on every request in 1.0 (operator, 2026-11-18 21:38 CT; a port reading of the operator's 22:40 CT message keeps this key for 1.0), and the hub derives the machine from the verified key.
- From R0: node-to-hub authentication stays behind one seam at the hub front door (crates/gdaemon/src/front_door/auth.rs). Identity comes from the node credential, never from the request body, so a per-node keypair or mutual TLS can replace the bearer key without changing node request bodies (a port reading of the operator's 2026-11-18 22:40 CT message).
- From R0, for NODEHUB and S4-NODE: a hosted node option needs tenant-scoped keys, key rotation and a public-CA certificate; none is built for 1.0. The operator's 2026-11-18 22:40 CT message says "If I ever sell a cloud hosted option I will have nodes on machines I don't control"; the requirements are a port reading of it.
<!-- gport:end found:NODEHUB -->

### S4-NODE

<!-- gport:kept found:S4-NODE -->
- From R0, optional: store the node key in the OS keystore (a port reading of the operator's 2026-11-18 22:40 CT message).
- From R0: the local-hop credential stays separate from the hub credential. This is a port-session decision, not an operator ruling; S4-NODE owns it.
<!-- gport:end found:S4-NODE -->
